### Settled

- **Sixth remedy (2026-10-18): one slot-holder predicate, hash-tagged per-pod keys, and A27 accepted.** DECISION remedy.slot-holder-predicate.2026-10-18, applying the owner's answers of 2026-10-18 to the spec recheck round 25 `redesign` stop. (1) A session holds a slot on pod P exactly when its row is non-terminal and names P. The predicate is stated once, as the **Slot-holder predicate** paragraph with the case table, and the seed, `ReserveSlotUnderLock`, `PodHasActiveSession`, `ListPodsWithActiveSlots`, and the replay filter cite it. The failure funnel's `resume_pending` and `awaiting_client_action` mutators clear `pod_assignment` in the edge write and drop the `rep.PodAssignment` stamp, and `persistPodAssignment` skips a recovery-state row (CODE-1 item 9, summary Decision 22). Row O20 and O17's funnel and suspension carve-out are deleted; O4, O10, Decision 21, and DOCS-1 item 9 now hold. `ClassifySessions` returns each non-terminal session's pod, and the rebuild replays a failed release on P unless the session holds a slot on P. The failed `/start` keeps its `ready` row naming the pod and stays a holder, because its retry binds without a reservation and its terminal release is keyed; the resume compensations release a pod no row names before the commit. Readers checked: no reader needs the failed pod's name (the resume driver claims a fresh pod; the start, rebuild, and finalize gates already branch on `IsRecovery`; `reportGuard` and `holdsPod` read the pod only on `suspended`; the Sweeper adopts recovery rows without a readopt). The public, admin, and tree views report no pod for a recovery row, as documented; the usage rollup's `podMinutes` becomes zero for a session that ends from a recovery state, filed as a summary Defect. (2) CODE-1 item 7 hash-tags the pod identifier in every per-pod key, reading §12.4's `lenny:pod:{pod_id}:*` braces as a literal tag (as for `{root_session_id}`), so `reserveScript`, `seedScript`, `releaseScript`, `rebuildScript`, and `Reset`'s three-key `DEL` stay in one slot. No migration: the untagged keys have no TTL and are never read again, the tagged ones rehydrate on first use, and the rename retires the stale string-key cleanup (old item 7, Decision 16, the `WRONGTYPE` watch-out). The other stores' cross-slot calls are a summary Defect that REC-1 item 6 files. No Redis Cluster fixture exists, so the test is a tier-1 hook-and-CRC16 check. (3) A27 is exactly four cases: the releasing replica exits before Redis returns; a release that failed on a non-Redis error; attempts that hit their deadline before the outage clock was stamped; and the cross-replica window between `ClassifySessions` and the script. The owner accepted the first earlier on 2026-10-18 and the other three on 2026-10-18 after this remedy narrowed them; the bound is Counted, ending with the GC within `claimOrphanTimeout` plus one sweep after the pod's last live session ends. The replay that empties a set without a disposition moved to new row A28 (GC, as A20). WATCHOUT: do not widen A27 without asking the owner (summary watch-out). (4) The outage mechanism restatements (summary What changes, Decision 21, the reseed Non-goal, the A27 edge bullet, CODE-1 item 8's coverage bullet, the code-level outage case, and the tier-1 rebuild bullet) are reduced to citations of the case table; DOCS-1 item 9 is the one reader-facing restatement and names only register-row outcomes. (5) OPENs closed: Redis Cluster CROSSSLOT (item 7); compensation_loss_test.go (it releases each reservation once and reads no slot key, so it keeps its assertions; Retargets names it); BUILD-GAPS P1 owner lines (REC-1 item 9 re-points the seven, and item 7 records F-4.9.37 and F-5.2.50); hidden-commit AfterRevokeWrite drain (no other path drains a terminal session's inbox or DLQ: no TTL, the DLQ sweep lists recovery states only; filed as a summary Defect for P5b); in-memory lease store hook (wired only with the in-process assigner, which the chart does not use; a C3 concern for P5b); DOCS-1 item 9 and A27's cases, and the A27 alive-replica flag (settled by the owner's acceptance). The independent review (remedy.slot-holder-predicate.2026-10-18.review) found that `MarkBudgetUnrecoverable` (`delegationbudget/adapters.go`) moves a bound root to `awaiting_client_action` without a release or a clear, and `releaseEarlierBinding` later releases it on a `resume_pending` row that still names the pod: `releaseEarlierBinding` now clears `pod_assignment` under the lock before its release (CODE-1 item 9, row F11, a tier-1 case), rather than widening A27. It also corrected O5's replay filter, CODE-1 item 8's empty-set citation (A28), and two O20 pointers in this section. SUPERSEDED IN PART by remedy.terminal-member-sweep.2026-10-18 (owner decision C): the replay filter and `ClassifySessions` are gone, the sweep reads row terminality rather than the predicate, and A27 is now the single owner-chosen clause "non-terminal, non-holder members stay counted"; its four cases above are retired. SUPERSEDED AGAIN by remedy.add-only-rebuild.2026-10-19: the sweep is gone, and A27 reads "a member whose release failed during an outage stays counted until a later release of it or the orphan GC after the pod's last live session".

- **Add-only recovery rebuild (replaces the terminal-member sweep).** DECISION remedy.add-only-rebuild.2026-10-19 (owner decision of 2026-10-19, revising option C of 2026-10-18). The outage ledger holds only add-only entries: fallback admissions and leaked marks, as a set; a clean failed release records nothing, and any `Release` that fails because Redis is unreachable stamps the clock. The rebuild reads the ledger, lists pods (`ListPodsWithActiveSlots`), and runs one `rebuildScript` per pod the ledger or the listing names (SADD the ledger's entries; DEL the flag of a listed pod). It removes no member and runs no disposition. Deleted with the sweep: `rebuildSweepScript`, `TerminalSessions`, the add-before-read ordering, and register rows A29 and A30 (A29's case is now an A27 case; A30's removal no longer occurs). Kept from option C: no failed-release replay and no failed-release ledger entries. Residue: A27, owner-accepted on 2026-10-19 (a member whose release failed during an outage stays counted until a later release of it or the orphan GC after the pod's last live session), A28 (rows O1 and O11: the admitting replica exits before its rebuild), and A31 (rows O15 and O16: a rebuild re-adds a fallback-admitted session whose release ran first; added by the review of 59548b902). The **Outage ledger and add-only rebuild.** paragraph beside the case table is the only statement of the ledger and the rebuild; CODE-1 item 8 cites it. WATCHOUT: do not reintroduce a removal at the rebuild, whether by row terminality, by the **Slot-holder predicate**, or by the age of the terminal write; the owner rejected each. CORRECTED by owner.stale-slot-reaper.2026-10-19: the A27 residue is now released by the stale-slot reaper once its session has been terminal for longer than `claimOrphanTimeout`; the orphan GC after the pod's last live session bounds only what the reaper keeps (register row A32).
- **Slot-set outage recovery case table (O1 to O19).** Row O20, appended by spec.25.fix-G1.1, was deleted by the sixth remedy (2026-10-18); O1 to O19 keep their numbers. DECISION remedy.outage-recovery-table.2026-10-18 (owner remedy 5, owner decision of 2026-10-18): the rebuild MERGES. `seedScript` drops its `DEL`, so every rehydration adds the rows' identifiers and removes no member (SPEC-1b's third substitution now reads "adds those identifiers to `lenny:pod:{pod_id}:active_slots`, keeping the members the set already holds"). The late-row case is closed by a per-`Counter` outage ledger: `fallbackReserve` records the admitted pod and session, and `rebuildAfterOutage` runs `rebuildScript` per pod (SADD the ledger's sessions; DEL the flag only for listed pods), clearing the clock only when the ledger is empty. The table in non-spec-changes.md is the ONLY rule site for slot-set outage recovery: rows are triggers (O1 to O7), replica views (O8 to O11), and member kinds (O12 to O19), each with a replace-seed and a merge-seed effect and a register row. Regenerated from it: CODE-1 items 1, 2, and 8; register rows A4, A11, A12, A13, A26, and new A27 (the stale member, owner's residue); the **Counted** backstop; the code-level case **A reservation made during a Redis outage**; DOCS-1 items 3 and 9; the CODE-1 tier-1 rebuild bullet, the tier-4 outage-recovery case, and the tier-8 restart, mid-rehydration, mid-rebuild, and failed-release cases; summary Decision 21, the reseed Non-goal, **What changes**, and a summary watch-out; spec-changes edge cases **A reseed drops leaked members and unpersisted reservations** (now restart and whole-pod reset only, and naming the resume), **A failed release the recovery rebuild does not replay** (A27, renamed by remedy.failed-release-replay.2026-10-18), and **Fast-path enforcement before the rebuild after a Redis outage** (A26). WATCHOUT: a change to outage recovery starts at its O row, then CODE-1 item 8, the register rows, and the tests; never restate a row's effect elsewhere. CORRECTED by remedy.add-only-rebuild.2026-10-19: O17 is any member whose session's row is terminal and O18 any member whose row is non-terminal and names another pod or none, and both are kept (A27); the edge bullet is **A member whose release failed during a Redis outage stays counted** and points at A27.
  CORRECTED by remedy.prune.2026-10-19: the table has one **Effect** column; the Replace-seed column is gone, and the live rules it held (row O1's uncounted release, and the cells the Merge-seed column inherited) are inlined in the Effect cells. Rows O12 to O14 are removed, and their numbers stay unassigned; their members are covered by the **Outage ledger and add-only rebuild.** paragraph's rule "The rebuild removes no member", which A11, A12, the spec-changes reseed bullet, and the tests now cite.
- **Outage rebuild: rejected alternatives.** Moved here from summary Decision 21 by remedy.prune.2026-10-19; the summary points here. Rejected: option C's terminal-member sweep, which removed every member whose session's row was terminal, because it removed a member before the session's own release had shut its runtime down and dropped a leak whose mark an exited replica held, so the pod admitted over its cap, which fails open; the same sweep limited to sessions whose terminal write is older than one coordination Sweeper listing plus the per-slot cleanup floor (20 seconds by default), because that age is an arbitrary timing heuristic and leaves the lost-mark and unperformed-release cases open; option A, the replay from each replica's ledger of the releases that failed during the outage, because one replica's replay removes another replica's live re-reservation of the same session, which fails open with no bound in time, and because the replay's per-(pod, session) keying and its retry protocol kept producing defects; option B, the same replay limited to sessions whose row is terminal or names another pod, because it keeps that ledger and its retry protocol; a replacing rebuild, because it drops `leaked:` members and in-flight reservations once per observing replica; a rebuild on the admitted session's next slot call, because that call is the session's own release; a rebuild at gateway start, because it runs on every rollout and cannot see the admissions of the process that exited; and a Postgres check before each occupancy-zero disposition, because it adds a store read to the emptying release and still lets the pod admit over its cap. Do not re-propose any of them without a new owner decision. The owner's stale-slot reaper of 2026-10-19 (summary Decision 23) is such a decision. It differs from option C's sweep in that it sends the adapter `Shutdown` and requires a clean answer before it removes a member, and it uses `claimOrphanTimeout` rather than a 20-second window.
- **Edge bullets cite their register rows.** DECISION spec.25.fix-G1.1: the spec-changes bullets **A reseed drops leaked members and unpersisted reservations**, **A failed release the recovery rebuild does not replay**, and **Fast-path enforcement before the rebuild after a Redis outage** keep their headings verbatim and one mechanism sentence and point at A11 and A12, A27, and A26 for cases and bound (the A13 and A15 precedent of spec.19.fix-G1.1). The A27 bullet now states only that the rebuild replays in the case table's cases and points at A27 for the rest. CORRECTED by remedy.add-only-rebuild.2026-10-19: the A27 bullet is now **A member whose release failed during a Redis outage stays counted** and points at A27 only.
- **Merge equals replace after a restart.** A Redis restart and the §10.1.4 reset both empty the set first, so "keeping the members the set already holds" only changes behaviour after the outage-end flag delete; SPEC-1a's self-seeding release, its "returned once" qualifier, and SPEC-1c, 1d, and 1f read consistently with the merge (spec.25.review-mechanism.1). SPEC-1a's "runs only on a release that removed the session's member and left the set empty" is a necessary condition, and no rebuild removes a member, so no rebuild empties a set (A28 is the uncounted release after the admitting replica exits, rows O1 and O11).
- **Release-path failure-outcome table (F1 to F20).** DECISION remedy.failure-outcomes.2026-10-18 (owner remedy 1): the only rule site for what a release path does when one of its steps fails. Rows are the release paths (`SlotClaimer.ReleaseSlot`, `releaseSlotRetrying` with `ReleaseSlotReservation`, `Binder.ReleaseSlot`, `Binder.Release`, `ReclaimClaimed`, the hook's step 5, D6, the failure funnel, `ReleaseUncommitted`, the resume compensation, `releaseEarlierBinding`, the suspension release, full_revoke steps 2, 3, 4(a), and 4(b), the `reserveSlot` rollbacks, the acquisition undos D9 and D10, the exclusive bind failures D15, and `rollbackBinding` with `releaseSingleShot`). Columns are context end, lock-wait error, store error, committed-then-error, superseded, and RPC cut; each cell gives action, return, and register row, with a row-level **Caller** column. Regenerated from it: register rows A4, A5, and A9, the A23 and A25 edge sites, the CODE-2 failure-outcome test (F4, F5), the CODE-6 writer-context case (F6, A5), the CODE-7 lead, 4(a) error, and `ReadPrimary` cases (F13 to F16), and the CODE-1 `podclaim` rollback case (A4); Races items 2 to 4 cite A17 to A19, the full_revoke post-row bullets cite A22 and F14, summary Decision 15 cites CODE-5 item 2, and the no-retry watch-out cites F2. The independent review then split F4's context-end cell (recycle patch detached; retire `Get` and delete on a context created after `shutdownAdapter`), named `rollbackBinding` in F3's caller cell, and added F3, F9, and F10 to A4 and A9.
- **CODE-6 step 5 context.** Step 5 keeps the writer's context. On `maxConcurrentSessions` above 1 a cut `Shutdown` releases the slot as leaked (counted, fail closed); on an exclusive pool CODE-2 item 8 writes the claim on a detached context; A5 bounds every caller-context end. The lock wait runs on `s.lifetime()`; two CODE-6 tier-1 cases pin it with the funnel holding the lock (writer ctx cancelled during the wait: release runs once after unlock; lifetime ctx cancelled: no `terminalReclaimPreRunning` or executor call).
- **Redis-outage rebuild.** DECISION owner remedy 2 (answer of 2026-10-18), CODE-1 item 8, summary Decision 21; A14 retired. CORRECTED by non-spec.11.fix-G1.1: only `fallbackReserve` (as in the tree) and an unreachable `Counter.RunRecovery` tick (modelled on `storagequota.RecoveryReconciler`, 2-second default, flag-tunable, started on `w.watchdogCtx`) stamp the outage clock; CORRECTED by remedy.failed-release-replay.2026-10-18: a `Release` that finds Redis unreachable also stamps it. CORRECTED by remedy.outage-recovery-table.2026-10-18: the reseed MERGES rather than replaces, the ledger counts late-row admissions, and the mechanism and its residue are stated only in the **Slot-set outage recovery case table** (see the first **Settled** entry). In the tree today `clearOutage` only zeroes `outageSince`, `fallbackReserve` adds no member, and `ReserveSlotUnderLock` counts rows only. CORRECTED by remedy.add-only-rebuild.2026-10-19: the rebuild adds the ledger's entries and removes no member; it replays no release.
- **Flag lifetime in spec (spec round 22).** SPEC-1b's fifth substitution makes §5.2 **Post-recovery rehydration atomicity.** the single home of the `rehydrated` flag lifetime: a Redis restart clears it, the §10.1.4 whole-pod reset deletes it (as `Counter.Reset` does), and the gateway deletes it for each pod a non-terminal session names when a Redis outage ends (citing §12.4); its parenthetical reads "(indicating the set must be rehydrated)". SPEC-1c's second substitution cuts the §12.4 `rehydrated` key row to "from Postgres. No TTL." under its existing §5.2 pointer. "Non-terminal session" and CODE-1's `best-effort-per-slot` filter select the same pods (only `mcs >1` pods carry a flag). No test pins the replaced strings.

- **Accepted-departure register (A1 to A35; A14, A29, and A30 retired; A32 to A35 added with the stale-slot reaper).** DECISION remedy.departure-register.2026-10-18: the only rule site for accepted departures. CORRECTED by owner.stale-slot-reaper.2026-10-19: the register now runs to A34 (A32 reaper keeps, A33 overlapping-`Shutdown` leak, A34 bind in flight past the window), and the **Counted** backstop names the reaper.
- **Post-terminal timing (remedy.reaper-age-table.2026-10-19).** The non-spec **Post-terminal slot activity table** (rows T1 to T7) is the only rule site for which activities can still act on a session's slot after its terminal write; its **Invariant** is `claimOrphanTimeout` ≥ the largest of the launch bound (`--max-finalizing-state-timeout-seconds`, T1), `--max-resuming-seconds` (T3), and `--workspace-seal-max-duration-seconds` (T4) plus one second; SPEC-1a is its one spec statement; SPEC-1l raises the default to 15 minutes; one chart value, `controller.claimOrphanTimeoutSeconds`, feeds the controller's `--claim-orphan-timeout` and the gateway's `--slot-reaper-terminal-age`, and the gateway and the chart refuse a violating value (CODE-9 items 6 and 7). Finalize (T2) does no pod work on a concurrent-workspace pool; T5 to T7 only release, and their overlap residue is A33. A34 (launch, restore) and A35 (seal) are misconfiguration-only: replica clocks that disagree by more than the margin. Rejected: a second reaper window per activity (a timing heuristic beyond the orphan timeout, barred by the owner); keeping 5 minutes and accepting the seal and launch residues (seal loss and a fail-open slot, barred by the owner); an adapter record that refuses a bind after an `absent` answer (it also refuses the `/start` retry after a failed launch whose reclaim found no entry); per-window operator invariants in flag text alone (the round-30 and round-32 sites drifted, and nothing enforced them); bounding the launch by `claimOrphanTimeout` itself (leaves the writer's stamp-to-commit gap); `--max-ready-state-timeout-seconds` as the launch bound (can cut a launch whose setup uses the full `setupTimeoutSeconds`).
- **full_revoke fence against the own-Sweeper race (Decision 19).** **Order** step 3 opens each listed session's terminal claim through `BeginTerminal` before its cancel write; a write that does not apply or errors ends it at once; step 4 ends every claim after 4(a) and before 4(b), and the handler ends them on every return path. `startRelease` refuses while `claims > 0`, so neither `sweepTerminal` nor the resume tail can release an R2 binding before 4(a); `beginSuspension` and `releaseUnderSlotLock` refuse on `claimed`; the funnel, the hook, `rollbackBinding`, `releaseEarlierBinding`, and `ReleaseUncommitted` are ungated but none acts on a `ready` R2 binding; `/start`'s refused `commitStart` leaves the binding registered and releases nothing. `terminalClaimRecord.open` is refcounted with an idempotent `sync.Once` end, and the record is per-replica in-memory state.
- **Departure-register cleanup.** D11's cells cite the full_revoke release table; the Binding legend no longer counts D11. The only binding publishers are `registerBinding` (`/start`), `create_and_start.go` and `delegated_child_materialize.go` after their running commits, `resume_driver.go`, and the coordination takeover seam; no finalize path publishes, so R2's "SDK-warm preConnect pod attached at finalize" clause was deleted. The single-shot translator release (D3a) publishes through `CreateAndStartService`.
- **R5 and R6 mint after step 2 (A22).** Proactive renewal cannot mint after step 2 (`credRenewalWiring.Renew` returns `ErrLeaseReleased` and releases a replacement whose predecessor vanished), but a §4.9 fallback rotation admitted before step 2 mints a replacement with no re-check (`proxyFallbackRotator.Rotate`); A22 bounds it by the session's own release, and CODE-7 tests it.
- **Deferred items 1 to 5 discharged (2026-10-18).** TEST-1 **Tier 11 (docs).** lists SPEC-1g to SPEC-1j, the predicate-stating SPEC-2 edits, and the pointer edits (§4.6.3 link, pool-phrase absence); A11 to A14 with DOCS-1 item 9 and the tier-8 and tier-4 cases (the `SandboxClaimOrphanRateHigh` runbook cause declined); A3 annotates F-4.6.29 (the `failFinalizing` commit-with-store-error case); CODE-1 and CODE-3 **Inverted tests.** name `reserveslotonpod_test.go`'s two stamp-failure tests, the `finalize_race_test.go` "ready write store error" subtest, and the `credential_lookup_failclosed_internal_test.go` row, and CODE-1 item 6 names the two `mcpfabric` fakes; `Binder.Release` claim writes run on `detachedReleaseContext` (CODE-2 item 8, Decision 20), narrowing A5 to the best-effort `Shutdown`.
- **CODE-2 item 8 placement.** The retire path's detached context is created after `shutdownAdapter` returns (a plain retire `Shutdown` has deadline 0 and runs to the caller's deadline), and the tier-1 test asserts the Sandbox `Get` context's deadline is at least `slotCleanupFloor` after the fake `Shutdown` returned. The recycle branch needs no placement rule because `WriteRecyclingStatus` precedes its `Shutdown`.
- **Round-5 non-spec findings applied.** DOCS-1 items 1, 9, and 10; A10 declined with a register reason; the CODE-7 rotator case uses two revoked sessions with direct-delivery leases on one pool credential; D11 cut; the **Order** lead reworded; disaster-recovery.md item 9 renames "Slot counters" to slot sets.
- **A5 contents.** On `maxConcurrentSessions` above 1 a cut `Shutdown` leaks the slot (`leaked = err != nil || !cleanly`, D4c), still counts in `podsTerminated`, and the holder's Sweeper releases the binding after a lock-wait expiry (pinned in a `sessionserver` CODE-7 bullet whose case asserts a later `ReleaseTerminalBinding` releases once). `Binder.Release` and `ReleaseSlot` swallow every adapter `Shutdown` error, so after CODE-7 an adapter failure no longer reaches `podTerminationFailures` (judged not a finding).
- **SandboxClaim end-of-occupancy disposition table (D1 to D18b).** DECISION remedy.mechanism-table.2026-10-16, CORRECTED by spec.3.fix-G1.1: one code-derived table over pool kind, `recycle.enabled`, binding publication (**Binding** column, `published` or `unpublished`), and outcome; SPEC-2 was regenerated from it, including CODE-2 item 7 (`rollbackBinding` clears `Recycle` for an unregistered concurrent binding, D18b). Invariants 1 and 2 were deleted as copies of SPEC-2c and SPEC-1a (non-spec.1.fix-G2.1). D14's Claim cell cites A24; D10, D13, and D17 rely on the register's GC backstop definition.
- **F-4.6.30 bound.** The landed chart fix (merge 638fc3855) bounds GC-backed departures by `claimOrphanTimeout` plus one sweep interval; the GC classifies `bound` and `recycling` claims by `BindingStateTransitionTime` and empty-phase claims by creationTimestamp, and deletes only after `PodHasActiveSession` returns false.
- **full_revoke release table (R1 to R12).** DECISION remedy.full-revoke-table.2026-10-17: **Order** steps 1 to 4, **Release outcomes**, and rows over the pre-revoke state and binding location. CORRECTED by non-spec.5.fix-G1.1: the table is the only *full* statement of order and accounting; SPEC-3a steps 1 and 4 and SPEC-3b's lease-count sentence state parts normatively, so the intro drops SPEC-3 from its cite list and a change "starts at that table, then SPEC-3". The **Order** lead (reconcile 2026-10-18) says SPEC-3a step 1 states the return-then-deny order and its precedence over the cancel writes, step 4 states the reclaim's precedence, and no other proposal site restates the list.

The summary **Deliverable index** lists SPEC-1 to SPEC-3, CODE-1 to CODE-8, DOCS-1, TEST-1, and REC-1 once each, with files as the staging names them; its CODE-1 line names `sessionserver/resume_rebind.go` (item 9), `podsession/resume.go` (item 4), and item 6's direct callers and test fakes, even where CODE-1's Targets list omits them (see **Open**). CORRECTED by owner.stale-slot-reaper.2026-10-19: S13 is CODE-9 (on S1 and S6), S14 TEST-1 (adds S13), and S15 REC-1 (on S14); S4 DOCS-1 also depends on S13; S1 lands SPEC-1a to SPEC-1k.
- **Docs deliverable DOCS-1.** DOCS-1 names the passage on each page (state-machines.md, glossary.md, architecture.md, execution-modes.md, lifecycle.md, configuration.md, runtime-configuration.md, workspace-seal-stuck.md, security-principles.md, multi-tenancy.md, session-lifecycle.md, concepts.md, disaster-recovery.md, troubleshooting.md), with the reader-facing definition of a release that ends the occupancy without reuse placed once in execution-modes.md **Recycle lifecycle**. It carries the three spec-gate retargets and the state-machines.md want-string of `session_failure_outcome_doc_reconciliation_test.go`; the three `Concurrent` mirrors take one byte-identical cell. Every DOCS-1 anchor matches its page once with whitespace collapsed. recycle-lifecycle.svg stays unedited (its footer stays true).
- **CODE-8 comment edits.** Item 1 edits the `SandboxClaimSpec` comment (a verbatim SPEC-2a substitution of its deletion-step list; it keeps its `// spec: §4.6.3 (CRD field ownership), §5.2 (execution modes)` cite), the `SandboxClaimStatus.Phase`, `WriteDispositionStatus`, and `Failed` constant comments (cite `§4.6.3 (SandboxClaim.status.phase enumeration)` "on the SPEC-2d edits"), runs `make generate` (writes charts/lenny/crds only), and copies the CRD to `pkg/embedded/crds/` (byte-identical test). Item 2 rewrites the force-terminate comment to the SPEC-2s pointer wording; item 3 rewrites the `ShutdownRequest.recycle` and `ShutdownRecycle` comments to §4.6.3 and §4.6.1, with `make generate-proto` (Go bindings only; no SDK mirrors `ShutdownRequest`). Several anchors are hard-wrapped across comment lines.
- **Decision 15 evicts.** CODE-5 item 2 has one eviction branch for `running`, `input_required`, `suspended`, `resume_pending`, `resuming`, and `awaiting_client_action`; the tier-1 test asserts eviction and TEST-1 Tier 4 says "evicts".
- **Row D9 takes no code change.** **Races the table does not close** gains its race and REC-1 item 6 files it.
- **Retry jitter and metric label.** CODE-1 item 5's pause is an IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE with no jitter requirement (go-redis default retries, jittered 8 to 512 ms); the release-path `pool` label of `lenny_slot_rehydration_total` is an IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE in CODE-1 item 3 that the implementor settles.
- **CODE-2 Part A recycle axis.** The exclusive column runs on both `recycle.enabled` settings (D1 on a recycling exclusive pool discriminates); on concurrent columns the axis tests a constant because `ReleaseSlotReservation` hard-codes `recycle=false`.
- **D10 filing.** D10 is a GC-bounded departure filed by REC-1 item 5 and one summary Defects entry, which REC-1 item 6 excludes; Decision 11 covers N2 only. The `claimPod` doc comment "A failure here leaves no claim behind" is already false because of D10.
- **Funnel order.** `applyFailureFromActive` writes the `resume_pending` or `awaiting_client_action` edge first and then runs `accountFailedSlot` and the failed-disposition release under `slotAccountLocks`.
- **remedy.review findings.** R5 adds D3c; R11 and R12 give D14 on `mcs 1` and D6a or D6b on `mcs >1`; **Release outcomes** adds the open terminal-writer claim to the refused `startRelease` case; the **Pool slot** column notes the N4 per-replica count; DOCS-1's definition adds "when the pool does not recycle"; DOCS-1 item 6 adds the glossary pod-reuse entry.
- **SPEC-1 vocabulary scope.** Text that defines REG-SLOTCOUNT as an atomic counter or describes INCR, DECR, decrement, or reset-to-0 is edited (SPEC-1g, 1h, 1i, 1j); nominal labels stay because they read true of a set's cardinality.
- **Release-path self-seed.** A release that finds the `rehydrated` flag absent rehydrates with its own member added to the seed and then removes it (`releaseScript` returns {-2,0}; `Release` gains `rehydrated`). A release that waits on a peer's lock, or finds that a reservation already seeded, removes nothing and skips the disposition; every interleaving traced still runs the occupancy-zero disposition exactly once where the set empties.
- **Leaked-slot lifetime home.** §6.2 **`leaked` slot semantics.** is the single home; SPEC-1a's leaked-lifetime sentence was deleted, and the reseed loss is a departure (A11).
- **Reservation checks membership first.** SPEC-1a and `reserveScript` check SISMEMBER before the cap; the tier-1 row "including at the cap" discriminates.
- **Redis-restart departures.** A Redis restart or the whole-pod reset empties the set, so the rehydration drops leaked members and unpersisted reservations (A11, A12), and a duplicate release after a restart or a flag delete can re-run the disposition (fails closed, A13); SPEC-1a's "returned once" carries "while Redis keeps the set and its `rehydrated` flag" (independent review of remedy.outage-recovery-table), and Decision 3 carries "while Redis keeps the set". The outage rebuild drops neither (case table rows O13, O14). They land in the register, REC-1, and docs, never as spec rules.
- **Shipped counter behaviour.** Today's `releaseScript` returns 0 on an absent key (a post-restart release runs the disposition under live siblings); a leaked release skips Redis entirely, whereas CODE-1 writes `leaked:<sid>`, so a leaked release can now fail during an outage (benign: the member stays counted).
- **Reservations precede the row.** `claimAtCreate` reserves before the INSERT sets `pod_assignment`, and `reserveResumeSlot` runs right after `claimPod` while the resume commit writes `pod_assignment`, so `GetActiveSlotsByPod` misses both in flight (the resume window spans the whole resume). Suspend release clears `pod_assignment`, so a reseed holds no member for a suspended session.
- **Counter.Reset.** Deletes `active_slots`, `rehydrated`, and `rehydrating` together and has no production caller, so the §10.1.4 reset is spec-only and flags outlive their pods.
- **Slot-set cost.** O(1) set operations on the same per-pod key, one reserve and one release script per session (about 400 Lua calls/s at 200 sessions/s against the ~6,500/s §12.4 budget), at most one `GetActiveSlotsByPod` per pod per flag loss on one replica, an emptied set deleted by Redis; SPEC-2 adds no etcd writes (a delete replaces the WarmPoolController's termination delete and SPEC-2d removes a status patch). After a Redis restart a release now reads Postgres; with a simultaneous Postgres failover it takes A4.
- **active_slots readers.** Only `pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter` reads or writes the key; the rehydration query uses the 0080 partial index; the §5.2 `WHERE state = 'active'` index note predates 0093.
- **go-redis re-send.** v9.19.0 re-sends after EOF or a read timeout, so an EVALSHA can run twice; the lost-reply release is in the edge case "A disposition failure after the member is removed" and CODE-1 item 5. `isRedisUnavailable` treats any `net.Error` and "i/o timeout" as unavailable.
- **Replacement trigger ignores the Redis set.** The fail-or-leak drain threshold reads the gateway's in-process `slothealth.Tracker` (failed events and leaked slots per pod, fed by `SlotBindError.Leaked`) plus the scrub-report leaked-pod drain stamp, so a reseed that drops `leaked:` members (A11) relaxes capacity only. `lenny_adapter_leaked_slots` comes from the same tracker.
- **Rehydration metric wiring.** CODE-1 item 4 sets `OnRehydrate: b.Rehydration` on every `podsession` `SlotClaimer` construction; `lenny_slot_rehydration_total` has no §16 or docs metrics row (pre-existing).
- **CODE-1 rollbacks and errors.** Item 4 routes the post-CREATE `reserveSlot` rollbacks through `ReleaseSlot(rctx, sb.Name, req.SessionID, false, false)`; the CREATE-loser withdrawal stays a raw `Counter.Release`. `ErrSlotCounterRelease` is a `podclaim` sentinel, so `slotcounter` tests assert only the raw source error and the wrapping is pinned at `podclaim`.
- **SPEC-2c home and predicate.** The §4.6.3 SandboxClaim row is the single home of which release deletes the claim: every exclusive release ends the occupancy, a concurrent one at slot occupancy zero (§5.2); it ends without reuse when the pool does not recycle, when no gateway replica published the session's binding (§7.3, with an in-place appositive defining publication), or on `maxConcurrentSessions: 1` when the session failed or crashed. "A slot reservation withdrawn after its claim `CREATE` fails (§4.6.1) ends no occupancy."
- **Binding publication in the tree.** `publishBinding` runs after `launchOnPod` on `/start` (through `registerBinding`, before `commitStart`), after the early running commit on create-and-start and delegated-child materialization, and after the resume commit (a bare `podRegistry.Put` on the checkpoint branch). `pod_assignment` is written at claim time and is no proxy. A `/start` refused at `commitStart` leaves the binding registered.
- **Occupancy projection.** A pure function of the current claim and the Sandbox phase: with no claim, a `claimed` pod drains, a `reserved` pod goes `idle`, and a warm-fill pod is left alone; an empty-phase claim also leaves it alone; a `failed` or `released` binding drains from any phase. The only gateway `failed` writes are the recycle boundary's `WriteDispositionStatus` callers.
- **SPEC-2d.** `failed` means scrub-failure retirement only; the tree's failed or crashed exclusive release deletes the claim and never writes `failed`.
- **Pre-projection departure (A15).** A release delete that lands while the Sandbox still reads `idle` or `reserved` leaves the pod `idle` with its `lenny.dev/tenant-id` label, a §5.2 pinned idle pod; the edge case points at §5.2 **Pinned idle inventory.** and **Deployer acknowledgment (runtime process kept across sessions).**. The label is admission-immutable and stamped on every pool kind right after the claim CREATE (a stamp failure fails the acquisition); every surviving joiner ran its own stamp.
- **Site classification membership.** A site is listed only when it contradicts §4.6.3 outright once SPEC-2c and SPEC-2d land; out of scope holds only §6.2 **Pre-attached failure retry policy:** (owner 0085; the summary 0085 row carries the re-baseline).
- **SPEC-2 pointer edits.** SPEC-2h (§6.2 recycle edges, label, **Concurrent pod lifecycle**), 2i (§28.5.1 drops "with the `failed` disposition"), 2j and 2k (§29.4 steps 17 and 12), 2l (§7.2 step 3), 2m (§4.7 `Shutdown` row), 2n (§6.2 `resuming → cancelled`), 2o (§7.1 `scrubPolicy`), 2p (§4.9), 2q (§8.3), 2r (§15.1 `created`), 2s (§24.11 force-terminate), 2t (§5.2 `Concurrent` preset), and 2u (§28.8 `CH-ATTACH` matrix cell: "names no retirement-counter reason for the retirement of a pod serving one session whose session failed or crashed"; counter reasons are only `session_count_limit` and `scrub_failure_limit`).
- **SPEC-2 lead wording.** "An edit that states the predicate uses the one wording "ends the occupancy without reuse", and no edit states the pod's resulting phase." The wording appears in 2a, 2b, 2c, 2e, 2f (across a wrap), 2g, 2h, 2j, 2k (across a wrap), 2m, 2o, and 2t; 2l, 2n, and 2p to 2s are bare `#463` pointers; 2i and 2u carry neither.
- **Sites deliberately not edited.** The §6.1 preConnect row; §5.2 **Scrub model.**; the §4.7 `ReportPodScrub` row (and its docs mirrors adapter-contract.md:86 and why-lenny.md:100); the §5.2 `Pod reuse` and `Bounded cohort` rows; the §7.2 step-3 tail; the §7.1 atomicity paragraph and diagram line; §6.2 suspension step 2; the §5.2 scrub-table closing sentence; the §6.2 `reserved ──→ idle` label and **Partial occupancy.**; SPEC-2c's §4.6.1 withdrawal citation; SPEC-2a's "§4.6 CRD table" label; the §16 orphan-alert cause list; the §15.1 `cancelled` row; the §5.2 burst sentence (scoped "After a Redis restart"; its "first slot allocation attempt" omits SPEC-1a's rehydrating release but still means one query per pod per flag loss, so it is incomplete rather than false, spec.26.review-performance.1).
- **Orphan GC, RBAC, and admission.** The gateway is the sole SandboxClaim spec, status, and delete writer (RBAC `create`, `get`, `delete`; `sandboxclaims/status` `get`, `patch`; one SSA field manager); `lenny-sandboxclaim-guard` registers CREATE only and data-residency CREATE and UPDATE, so no webhook sees a claim DELETE; the CRD has no CEL rules; SandboxClaim has no finalizer or ownerReference; only hold-expiry DELETEs carry preconditions.
- **§11.4 staging.** SPEC-3a step 1 returns every enumerated lease and then deny-lists, before the first cancel write; step 6 points at step 1; step 2 writes `cancelled` and releases the binding, dropping `USER_REVOKED`; step 4 reclaims for "a cancelled session that never started and whose pod binding the handling gateway replica does not hold ... before that replica's step-2 releases". SPEC-3b keeps "The reported lease count is the number of leases step 1 adds to the deny list" and omits the uncounted pre-running reclaim from the Note.
- **Why step 1 returns the lease.** Removing the shared Postgres lease row makes every replica's proxy miss `GetByToken` (LEASE_TOKEN_INVALID before the deny check and before the fallback flow), independent of pub/sub, and `Release` returns the pool slot; both `Release` implementations find a peer-minted lease through the shared store and no-op on an unknown ID; renewal's `Advance` never inserts.
- **Release-before-deny home.** CODE-7 item 7 releases every enumerated lease before the first deny-list write; summary Decision 8 and the P5b row state no order. The discriminating tier-1 case wires the real propagator with `directModeRevocationRotator` and two direct-delivery leases on one pool credential.
- **ReleaseRevokedBinding contract.** Its nil set means this replica owed no release (non-terminal row, refused `startRelease`, no binding, evicted stale binding); every other unreleased outcome is `(false, err)`, including the executor error and the lock-wait expiry (binding stays registered; the Sweeper retries on its next 15 s listing). Its outcome cases sit in a `sessionserver` CODE-7 test bullet because `slotAccountLocks` and `startRelease` are unexported. `terminateLocal` maps released to `PodsTerminated`, an error to `FailedSessions`, and `(false, nil)` to neither.
- **CODE-7 tests and context.** `callCtx = context.WithTimeout(context.WithoutCancel(ctx), userTerminateRPCTimeout)` per binding (20 s); step 4(a) also survives a client disconnect (`ReclaimClaimed` and `ReleaseSlotReservation` are detached). The admin tier-1 bullet reseeds `TestPartialFullRevokeFansOutOnlyTheCancelledSessions_spec_11_4` in `ready` (`RevokeUserLeases` receiving all three sessions discriminates), seeds a superseded row, and asserts that a superseded or failing write's claim ends before the next recorded call and that no `BeginTerminal` claim is open at return; `recordingRevokeHook` gains `BeginTerminal` and `ReclaimRevokedPreRunning`.
- **Credential facts.** Production uses `credassign.Client` (Token Service): `Release` is a synchronous `RevokeCredentials` RPC (5 s, breaker after 5 failures, 30 s cooldown) and removes the record whatever the outcome, emitting `token.revoked` "session_released"; the in-process `credassign.Service` is the §17.4 dev profile and never errors. The revoker and assigner share `w.llmLeases`. `denyList.Revoke` returns nothing, so `leasesRevoked` equals the enumerated count.
- **Deny-list revoke hook.** On an in-process assigner `Propagator.Revoke` runs `directModeRevocationRotator.onRevoke` synchronously: it marks the pool credential unselectable, lists `LeasesByCredential`, and dispatches `proxyFallbackRotator.Rotate`, which mints with `AssignProto` and pushes `RotateCredentials` with no session-state check.
- **USER_REVOKED and the response.** `USER_REVOKED` appears in spec/ only at §11.4 step 2; the adapter never reads `ShutdownRequest.reason`, and nothing sets it after CODE-7 item 6. The full_revoke response and audit keys are untyped Go map keys; the OpenAPI operation declares a bare `200 OK`; docs/api/admin.md names no fields; the error path answers `INTERNAL_ERROR` after running the fan-out for the cancelled IDs. The `lenny:session:terminate` request has no schema and no spec home beyond the §11.4 Note.
- **Sweeper and unbind.** The Sweeper lists every row each pass (15 s default) and hands a terminal row bound here to `ReleaseTerminalBinding`; `PodExecutor.Release` unbinds before the binder runs, so a release cut after the unbind leaves nothing for the Sweeper. `adapterclient.Shutdown` has no internal timeout.
- **Session identity facts.** Delegated children inherit the parent's `user_id`; `session.StateStarting` has no gateway writer; a `/start` binding carries generation at least 1, so D6's `bindGen == 0` arm never evicts it; `terminateLocal` matches only the cancelled `SessionIDs`.
- **§11.4 step citations.** Step 1 (sessionstore List), step 2 (propagator.go, billing_security_workers.go), step 6 (user_revocation.go, retargeted to step 1 by CODE-7 item 8), step 7 (interactionstore); CODE-7 targets name propagator.go, so item 8 reaches its package comment. No spec/ or docs/ site cites a §11.4 step number.
- **§11.4 Note enumeration.** One response key per listed effect; `AfterRevokeWrite` and the pre-running reclaim are uncounted and stay in the numbered steps. R4 (a materializing delegated child) names no pod, so step 4 has no object there.
- **Resume ordering.** `releaseEarlierBinding` runs before `resumeOnPod`, and the `resuming` write runs later in the entry hook; a stale resume-family binding exists only on a replica that lost a running-state takeover. `ReleaseUncommitted(requireReclaimed=true)` returns before `releaseResumeSlot` and `releaseAttemptCredentials` on both pool kinds, and `notLanded` only logs.
- **Tier-4 resume-family case.** Takeover, then a pod failure on the adopter, then `resuming`, then DELETE on the old coordinator; the assertion is no seal RPC through the stale binding.
- **Seal loss filed.** REC-1 item 4 annotates F-10.1.33 with a pointer to **A superseded coordinator's terminal write loses the seal**, owner P2; the loss is new with CODE-5.
- **Double-decrement home.** Summary Decision 17 (tested paths plus the under-count consequence); CODE-2's Depends line points at it; the CODE-2 tier-1 double-release test list stays a test list.
- **CODE-6 lock safety.** No deadlock: `transitionToFailed` unlocks before the hook, `applyFailureFromResuming` takes no lock, `completeResume` calls no hook, `slotAccountLocks` is per session, and D6 runs on its own goroutine. `sessionLocks.Lock` returns `ctx.Err()` and holds nothing when its context ends.
- **Finalize exits.** On `mcs >1` `ResolvePool` failure is the only prepare-failure exit and through-Prepare failures (D15) are exclusive only; plan parse precedes it on every pool kind; a second by-name delete after `failPhase` is benign. CODE-3 Part B asserts end states of the claim, slot set, and lease.
- **D8 and rollbackBinding tests.** Tier-1 `podclaim` constructs the set-emptying withdrawal against a winner in `bound`, `recycling`, and `reserved`; tier 7a asserts the loser makes no claim write. The `rollbackBinding` test asserts D18 and D18b by citation with "no `recycling` patch and no recycle `Shutdown`".
- **Harness checker.** The slot-set checker clause reads "keeps a `bound` claim", matching `BoundClaims`.
- **Tier-11 gates.** The spec breaks `claimOwnershipRowSentence` and `claimWriterSetCell`, the 0067 staged-block comparison (entries for SPEC-1e and SPEC-2g only; SPEC-1h, 2i, and 2u are outside 0067's block), and the §11.4 "publishes the step-2 `Shutdown` request" literal; DOCS-1 retargets them. `session_policy_table_mirror_reconciliation_test.go` compares the docs mirrors only with each other. Closing F-11.4.16 needs a `**Resolution` note for `TestSuspendResumeFiledFindingsAreFiledOnce_spec_7_2`.
- **Spec lane.** A spec-lane step runs no tier and commits only spec/, so S1 to S3 declare "Tiers 0." and the retargets ride in DOCS-1 at S4.
- **CODE-4 manifest.** CODE-4 deletes the `createdsweeper` move line in `scripts/refactor/manifest`; no chart value renders a created-sweeper flag; the watchdog's store-based sweep covers crashed replicas.
- **CODE-2 item 3 discriminator.** `claimAtCreate` and `scrubPolicyForPool` use the same `MaxConcurrentSessions > 1` condition on every create path.
- **Plan conformance.** The P1 entry is gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md §10.2 (around :2745) with drafting rules and the test instruction above it. Every deliverable is staged or declined (single-releaser rule: Non-goals; durable release record: Decision 7; owner check: Non-goals; C3: Decision 18 to P5b; F-4.9.34 retry: credential-accounting proposal; claim-register row in tests/claim-map.json: REC-1 lead; plan tick: REC-1 item 7). Downstream consumers: P5b ("P1's lease reclaimer"), P2 ("P1's release owner"), R21 (declined record).
- **adapterclient `Shutdown` comment staged.** The `Client.Shutdown` doc comment ("the §11.4 full_revoke fan-out passes `USER_REVOKED`") is staged under CODE-7 item 6 and its Targets, Files touched, and index line (remedy.outage-recovery-table.2026-10-18); the remaining `USER_REVOKED` mentions (users_invalidate.go, propagator.go) are Go comments only, and no wire schema names the reason.
- **A27 does not relax the retirement bound.** A kept stale member that holds a concurrent pod's occupancy above zero behaves like a `leaked` slot: §5.2 **Session count limit:** fires `maxSessionsPerPod` on the release that reaches the count, decoupled from occupancy zero. The merge and the SPEC-1a flag clause regress no security control (gateway-owned set, §12.4 Postgres fallback and fail-closed window kept).
- **Client surface of the spec edits.** SPEC-2d, 2m, 2r, 2s, and SPEC-3a and 3b are description or pointer changes with no field, enum, error-code, or RPC-name change; the SandboxClaim `failed` enum stays; REG-SLOTCOUNT is internal and no admin API exposes it.
- **P1 member coverage.** All 18 P1 member findings in the plan entry (F-4.6.26 to F-4.6.29, F-4.9.32 to F-4.9.35, F-5.2.49, F-7.3.33, F-7.3.34, F-10.1.21, F-10.1.28, F-10.1.30, F-10.1.32, F-11.4.10, F-11.4.11, and F-11.4.15) are named in the summary, problem statement, or staging; the plan's INV-1 to INV-7 assign P1 no spec deliverable.
- **Spec edit-site sweep (round 25).** Swept INCR, decrement, reset to zero, "naturally cleared", "slot counter", REG-SLOTCOUNT, "failed disposition", "default disposition", return-to-pool phrasings, `USER_REVOKED`, full_revoke, user invalidation, force-terminate, "occupancy reaches zero", and claim deletion; every hit is staged, under **Sites deliberately not edited**, residual naming, or out of scope. docs/ mentions slot rehydration only at disaster-recovery.md (DOCS-1 item 9), and no docs page states the flag lifetime.
- **Slot-holder predicate needs no spec edit.** No spec/ sentence says a `resume_pending` or `awaiting_client_action` row keeps naming the failed pod: §10.1.1 adopts those states regardless of pod, §10.1.2 step 2 skips the fence when no pod is bound, §7.2 **Exits from `resuming`.** names the replacement pod in no row field before the commit, and §6.2 and §7.3 have the resume driver claim a fresh pod (spec.26.review-docs-alignment.1, spec.26.review-fresh.1).
- **Usage rollup derivation.** `podMinutes` and `credentialLeaseMinutes` derive from the row's current `pod_assignment` (`resultrollup.go`); spec names `podMinutes` only in §8.8 examples, and billing `pod_minutes` (§11.2.1) does not use that rollup, so the summary Defects entry is its home rather than a spec edit (spec.26.review-docs-alignment.1).
- **Hash-tag notation.** CODE-1 item 7's reading of §12.4's `lenny:pod:{pod_id}:*` braces as a literal tag matches the spec's notation: §28.3 writes REG-SLOTCOUNT as `lenny:pod:<pod>:active_slots` and REG-COORDLEASE as `t:<tenant>:lease:session:<session>`, eliding the braces §12.4 uses for `t:{tenant_id}:`, so no §28.3 or §12.4 key cell needs an edit. The tree builds untagged keys today. A per-pod tag spreads pods across Cluster slots, and the scripts were already multi-key (set plus flag), so the tag adds no write (spec.26.review-fresh.1, spec.26.review-performance.1).
- **Binding publication has one definition site.** §7.3 never defines binding publication (its only mention is "no published binding" in the resume paragraph), so SPEC-2c's in-place appositive is the single definition rather than a copy (spec.26.review-single-source.1).
- **Mixed-version rollout is out of scope.** DECISION f11.out-of-scope-defects: the summary Defects entry **A gateway rollout across this change runs two slot structures for one pod.** (before **Races the disposition table does not close**) names §10.5's coexistence requirement, the chart's `RollingUpdate`, the untagged `INCR`/`DECR` counter, and the no-shim rule as the reason no fix is staged; a dual-read shim or an open decision was rejected. CODE-1 item 7's scope sentence and the **Keys written before this change** bullet stay true.
- **Firing-11 Defects entries.** The multi-key Redis commands that fail with `CROSSSLOT` in other stores, the full_revoke cancel write that skips `AfterRevokeWrite`, the usage rollup's `podMinutes`, and the mixed-version rollout each stand as out-of-scope entries under **Defects in the shipped tree that this proposal does not stage** (f11.cleanup). The summary section order is unchanged and the log has no `## Retired` section.
- **Open decisions for the human.** None. The owner answered the A29 and A30 decision on 2026-10-19 by choosing the add-only rebuild, which retired both rows; the earlier replay decision (non-spec.12.follow-up-fix.1) was closed by option C on 2026-10-18.

### Traps

- **Rule-site discipline.** The failure-outcome table, the accepted-departure register, the disposition table, the full_revoke release table, and the slot-set outage recovery case table are each the only rule site (the full_revoke table the only full one) for their content. A change starts at the row, re-derived from the functions it names, then moves to the register row and tests; cut copies to citations, never re-synchronise them. Copies drifted every time they existed (D11 against R5; Invariant 1; Races items 2 to 4 against A17 to A19; the A13 and A15 bullets; the summary fence watch-out; the outage rebuild restated across A11, A12, A26, CODE-1 item 8, and DOCS-1 item 9). Facet-by-facet rounds and per-site prose were tried and withdrawn.
- **Edge bullets stay pointers.** The reseed, A27, and A26 edge bullets drifted from their rows each time a row widened (A27's bullet lost the deadline-before-stamp and O15/O16 cases; A26's lost O11's admitting-replica-exit disjunct); spec.25 cut them to mechanism plus pointer. Do not add cases or bounds back, and do not file a bullet's omission of a register case as drift or as a missing edge case.
- **A27 bullet heading is load-bearing.** The A27 edge-site cell names the spec-changes bullet **A member whose release failed during a Redis outage stays counted** verbatim; renaming it needs that cell edited in the same change.
- **SPEC-1a and SPEC-1b agree under the slot-holder predicate.** SPEC-1a ("holds a slot") defines what the set represents, and SPEC-1b ("the pod's non-terminal sessions") defines the seed source; since the sixth remedy of 2026-10-18 a non-terminal row naming the pod is exactly a slot holder (**Slot-holder predicate**, CODE-1 item 9), and only rows O13 and O14 (members no row records) separate the set from the seed. Do not reword either spec edit; the predicate lives in the non-spec staging.
- **Funnel-released member: the O20 departure is withdrawn.** spec.25.fix-G1.1 and spec.25.fix-design-G1.1 recorded the member a funnel release left (`applyFailureFromActive` released the slot while the non-terminal row kept `pod_assignment`, so a merge rehydration re-added it) as case-table row O20 and an A27 case, rejecting both a seed narrowed to slot-holding states and a funnel clear of `pod_assignment` (GC and resume-driver blast radius). The owner chose the funnel clear (sixth remedy, summary Decision 22, CODE-1 item 9), and O20 and its A27 case are deleted. While Decision 22 stands, do not re-derive the O20 departure or narrow `GetActiveSlotsByPod` instead; the restart and whole-pod-reset variants predate 0093.
- **Rule-site naming is load-bearing.** The A11, A12, A13, A15, A26, and A27 register cells name the spec-changes bullet headings verbatim, and A16 to A19 and R3 cite Races items by position; renaming a heading or renumbering an item breaks them. Races item 2's "where today's rollback deletes no joined claim" is the marker the lead's "except where the item says otherwise" relies on and must stay. Never cite A14 outside the log.
- **Recovery rebuild placement and scope.** The rebuild must run before the fast-path script on a replica whose clock is set; keep `clearOutage` off the bare-answer path. Scope the flag delete to pods a non-terminal session names (cost). The rebuild changes a set only by its ledger SADD and its flag delete, so a test of row-listed members must drive a reservation or release before asserting set contents. Do not make the rebuild an eager SADD of listed rows with the flag kept: a release's SREM can land before that SADD and resurrect a terminal session's member; the flag delete makes releases wait on the blocking rehydration. Withdrawn: a replacing reseed (owner merge decision), a rebuild on the admitted session's next slot call (that call is its own release), a once-per-outage Redis marker, gating on "this replica admitted", stamping on every call, rebuild at start, and a Postgres check per disposition. The outage ledger is admissible only as a complement to the listing (it covers unwritten rows, which share the process's fate).
- **Spec sweep after a non-spec mechanism change.** Decision 21 claimed the rebuild was "SPEC-1d's sentence" and needed no spec edit; three spec sentences still made the flag restart-only, and every spec lens filed it in round 22. When a non-spec decision changes a mechanism the spec describes, grep spec/ for every statement of it, and do not restate CODE-level mechanics (clock, probe, listing) in spec. The add-only rebuild needs no spec statement beyond SPEC-1b's flag delete: SPEC-1d's "rebuilt through the standard blocking-rehydration path" and SPEC-1b's add-only seed are untouched, and the rebuild runs no disposition. A member it keeps after its session stopped holding a slot departs from SPEC-1a's one member per slot-holding session and is register row A27, stated by the spec-changes edge bullet; do not file the silence as a missing edit site.
- **slotcounter.go comment quotes replaced spec text.** pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go:208 quotes the §5.2 string "writes the accurate count ..., sets the" that SPEC-1b replaces. It is a CODE-1 comment fix for the non-spec loop, never a spec-loop finding; no test or gate pins any SPEC-1b replaced string.
- **Citer sweep reaches summary decisions (MISTAKE).** Round 3's design added a departure to the pre-running exclusive delete but its citer sweep missed summary Decision 5 and the rejected-alternative bullet on the exclusive-delete precondition, both saying the delete "always drains the pod"; both were reworded to "drains, apart from the departure ... records". When a change falsifies a universal, grep the summary's decisions and rejected-alternative bullets as well as the staging.
- **Redis restart semantics.** The chart runs Redis with `appendonly yes` on a PVC, so a restart may keep sets and flags; the spec frames a restart as data loss, and the CODE-1 clock and probe observe a restart as an outage. Do not treat the restart clause as the only trigger. A Sentinel failover that loses writes but keeps the flag triggers no reseed (the INCR counter had the same exposure; fails closed like A12).
- **CODE-6 step 5 alternatives withdrawn.** Running step 5 on `s.lifetime()` or `context.WithoutCancel` was rejected (`adapterclient.Shutdown` has no timeout); a bounded detached context was rejected (no bound fits both budgets). Some `AfterTerminalWrite` callers pass a request-derived ctx (`expireSession`, `afterFailed`, `recordSessionCompleted`) and others `WithoutCancel` (mcptools), so a discriminating test uses the former. Any lock-wait cancellation test must hold the lock before cancelling, because `sessionLocks.Lock`'s select picks at random when both cases are ready.
- **A25 fix withdrawn.** Re-reading after the cancel write's store error runs on the failing store and cannot tell this cancel from another writer's; a duplicate by-name reclaim can delete a successor's claim (A21); running 4(a) for every listed pre-running row deletes live rows' claims. A hidden-commit row holding a binding is still released by D6 only because step 3 ends the claim on any write error; keeping the claim open after an error would strand it. `pgtenant.InTx` can return a commit error after `fn` succeeded.
- **full_revoke fence traps.** The step-3 claims must end before 4(b), or `ReleaseRevokedBinding`'s own `startRelease` refuses. A leaked claim makes `startRelease` refuse that session on the handling replica for the process lifetime, so the superseded and store-error branches (absent from `[]RevokedSession`) need explicit ends. A step-4(a) reclaim with no deadline would hold off D6 indefinitely (check in CODE-7). Withdrawn fence alternatives: a departure, 4(a) skipping a started release, 4(a) after each cancel, holding `slotAccountLocks` across the write and drain.
- **Fence statements stay put (MISTAKE).** Round 7 deleted the admin test's claim-end assertion along with its restatement of **Order**, leaving superseded and failed writes unpinned; the remedy was a seeded case plus a leak oracle, not a restored restatement. Do not reintroduce a summary watch-out on when the claims open or end (the round-7 copy had already dropped the end-before-4(b) half). Decision 19 and CODE-7 item 3's doc-comment clause omit that half but do not contradict **Order**; keep `RevokedSession.EndClaim` and its gloss when trimming CODE-7 item 2.
- **Lease-return MISTAKE (spec round 1).** spec.1.fix-G3 moved the lease return off `RevokeUserLeases` and kept only the deny write, weakening the shipped fleet-wide durable rejection (peer proxies accept the token until the Sweeper releases; a binding-replica exit leaves it valid until ExpiresAt). Do not return to deny-only, add a revoked mark plus a proxy rule, or restore the bare `Remove` (strands the pool slot, F-11.4.10).
- **Per-lease interleaving reopens the rotation push.** Release A, deny A, Release B lets the deny for A find B on the same pool credential and rotate a fresh key onto a revoked session; every `Release` precedes the first deny write. A single-lease rotator test or `TestUserLeaseRevokerRevokesAndDenies` (distinct credentials, proxy delivery) cannot tell the orders apart. Do not add a per-session guard to `onRevoke`.
- **Step-1 crash and latency.** A crash after some `Release` calls and before any deny write cannot be repaired by a repeat (`LeasesBySession` no longer lists them); token rejection still holds through record removal. In Token Service mode the critical path waits on N serial RPCs (about 25 s before a hung service opens the breaker; a slow service costs N times latency); a concurrent `Release` fan-out is allowed if every Release finishes before the first deny write. Moving `Release` after the cancel writes needs a spec edit.
- **No "no release precedes its deny-list entry" guarantee.** A lease minted after the step-1 enumeration (in-flight start, fallback rotation, R5, R6) gets no entry; its token dies at the session's release (A22).
- **Deny-list granularity and session set are P5b's (C3).** Entries are per credential, so step 1 deny-lists a shared pool credential platform-wide and returns pool leases on it; full_revoke entries live in memory and the §4.9 startup rebuild never restores them; §4.9's populators omit full_revoke; an in-memory lease store lets a peer rotate a lease it minted; direct-mode slots return before the pods drop the key; SPEC-3b's narrowing to non-terminal sessions is the P5b session-set question. Do not file any of these against 0093.
- **Per-replica credential accounting (N4).** With the in-process `credassign.Service`, step 1's `Release` decrements the handling replica's count; `Client.Release` removes the record even when the RPC fails. Both predate 0093.
- **Do not renumber §11.4 steps.** interactionstore cites step 7.
- **Tree versus post-CODE-7 full_revoke.** Today the cancel writes commit, then the fan-out runs the pod terminate before `RevokeUserLeases` (deny then `Remove` per lease), with no pre-running reclaim; `DismissByUser` runs in `handleInvalidateUser`; the handler's store-error comment becomes false under CODE-7 item 1. Checking SPEC-3a or the table against HEAD shows a false mismatch.
- **§11.4 Note list is the counted list.** An added effect owes a response key and partial-failure detail. Withdrawn: an exemption clause, a reclaim count key, folding the reclaim into `podsTerminated`.
- **SPEC-3a step 4 wording.** Use "the handling gateway replica"; "before" names only that replica's releases because a peer's D6 can precede 4(a) (Races item 3). Withdrawn: dropping only "instead", "no binding on any replica" (false for R3).
- **Executor error surfacing.** `releaseReportedBinding` (failure.go, which CODE-6 says is unchanged) drops the executor error; CODE-7 item 4 works by inlining `releaseExecutor` or by changing that signature (three callers). A missing registry entry under the lock means "no binding" (nil), never a failure. Keep the "a later `ReleaseTerminalBinding` releases it once" assertion: it pins `endRelease` on the lock-wait error path.
- **Release self-seed.** A rehydrating release must seed its own member, or a post-terminal release removes nothing and Pass 1 reuses the unscrubbed pod. Withdrawn: "dispose when the rehydrated set is empty", dropping the `removed` gate, a phantom releaser member. Do not seed in `waitForFlag`, and do not file the peer-seeded release that removes nothing as a lost disposition.
- **Duplicate release after a Redis restart.** `WriteRecyclingStatus` patches any non-`recycling` phase, `reserved` included, though the state package admits no `reserved → recycling` edge; a duplicate post-restart release re-patches a held claim (re-scrub or retire) or deletes it, fail closed (A13). The resource-to-owner row "removes nothing" holds only while Redis keeps the set; do not add a per-row exception.
- **Pass 1 joins any live tenant claim.** `ClaimSlot` Pass 1 reserves onto any same-tenant claim not `released` or `failed`, including fresh empty-phase claims and `bound` claims on an empty set, and GC predicate 1 never fires once a new row names the pod. A skipped disposition leaves a joinable `bound` claim reused without the whole-pod scrub, and any by-name delete of a claim a sibling may have joined can drain a pod under it. No UID or resourceVersion precondition helps (a sibling reservation never writes the claim); do not re-file the by-name release delete as a missing precondition (Races item 1, A21).
- **No acquisition-undo universal (MISTAKE).** "Rollback" as a claim-delete edge licensed `reserveSlot`'s unguarded delete, and a later undo universal let the CREATE loser's withdrawal delete the winner's claim. The withdrawal stays a raw `Counter.Release`; D8 leaves the winner's claim intact; do not re-add a universal, even for the exclusive `claimIdle` delete. Reordering `reserveSlot` to CREATE first was withdrawn.
- **No pod outcome at a releasing site.** Do not write "retires", "drains", or "not reused" beside a claim delete; §4.6.1 **Occupancy projection** decides.
- **No unconditional recycling statement.** Do not restore "A recycling pool's release patches the claim to `recycling` instead" in §4.6.1; "after clean session termination" was a partial predicate copy that excluded a concurrent pool's failed last release, which recycles.
- **Publication predicate rather than §6.2 running.** The gateway cannot observe the adapter's record after an unknown start outcome. Withdrawn: reclassifying D18, D18b, and D14 as `running`; keying on the running commit; keying on `pod_assignment`; a separate definition site. "Pre-running" still names session states; do not change D3b or D4b to delete or add the publish-versus-`commitStart` order to the spec.
- **Pre-projection departure: do not restate §5.2.** Rounds 3 to 5 each summarized which acquisition admits, refuses, stamps, or retires the pinned pod and each was falsified (the fallback claim stamps no drain request; a failed stamp defers retirement). Withdrawn remedies: restoring the `failed` write, writing `failed` on every no-reuse release, a controller drain on an `idle` or `reserved`-level delete, waiting for the projection. Do not re-file SPEC-2d's delete as removing a level-triggered control (rounds 7, 12, 16, 18, 21 declined it).
- **Site classification by contradiction.** Do not re-add agreeing or departure-only sites; a missing-site finding must show an outright contradiction outside the departure window. Never classify a site out of scope with its own section as owner; convert it to a pointer edit. Sweep "recycle disposition" and "default disposition" too, and when a §28.5 card sentence changes, check the §28.8 matrix row for that channel (rounds 1 to 15 missed SPEC-2u's cell).
- **Pre-existing duplicates are not (g) findings.** The reserved-hold rule at §4.6.1 and §6.2, the §4.6 CRD row's trigger lists, the §4.6.1 **Pod claim mechanism** list, and §5.2's failure-retire sentence predate 0093; SPEC-2 adds only cited clauses.
- **Residual "slot counter" names are settled.** "Redis-counter occupancy" (spec/06), "Redis slot-counter occupancy" (06:166), §5.2 slot cleanup, §4.6.1, §12.4 (including the "Slot counter rehydration flag" label), §18, the `slotCounterPostgresFallbackMaxSeconds` key, chart comments "atomic slot counter", and the metric help text are naming; do not file them.
- **D10 and D9 by-name deletes.** `fallbackClaim`'s delete on stamp failure is no precedent for a `claimIdle` delete (both share the sibling-join hazard on `mcs >1`). Decision 11's old D10 reason was false on `mcs 1`; CODE-1 item 4 still cites "the reason Decision 11 gives", so keep Decision 11's wording general.
- **CODE-2 item 7 and the departure.** D18b's delete joins A15 in the pre-projection window although the tree recycled there at 85c9ab19a; judged too narrow to file.
- **Pre-existing findings to leave unfiled (spec.25).** A full_revoked proxy lease gets `LEASE_TOKEN_INVALID`, which the §15 error catalog omits, and §28.5 says `CREDENTIAL_REVOKED` only on a deny-list hit; today's deny-then-`Remove` already does this. A reservation whose reply is lost at outage onset can leave a member while the call takes the fallback, with no releaser; merge keeps it (counted, fails closed) and INCR had the same orphan. The rehydrating-lock TTL stale-seed race is the tree's. The §4.9 startup rebuild re-seeds only credentials with an active lease, which step 1 has removed (P5b's).
- **Races-list windows.** A Pass 1 join onto a `recycling` claim mid-scrub and a cross-replica `ready`-row D2 removing a running session's member predate 0093 and are owned (REC-1 item 6, R16).
- **Spec-lane red window.** A `spec-only` run leaves the three tier-11 gates red until S4 lands; placing the docs step between S2 and S3 breaks the spec prefix.
- **Checklist renumbering.** Ledger entries before reconcile 2026-10-18 use older step numbers (S4 CODE-1, S5 CODE-2, S6 CODE-3, S7 CODE-4, S10 CODE-7, S11 TEST-1); the current checklist is under **Checklist** in **Settled**.
- **Build-tagged tiers escape tier 0.** `runStaticTier` vets only untagged packages and `contract`; a signature change breaks `chaos`, `integration`, `component`, `load_local`, or `security` files silently until their tier runs (CODE-1 item 2's `Reserve` broke tests/tier8_chaos/slot_counter_redis_outage_test.go, hence S6 runs tier 8). Grep tagged tiers when a step changes a signature.
- **Supersession chaining.** The 0093 REG-CLAIM entry must follow 0079's and use 0079's `to` as its `from`.
- **Failure-outcome doc pin.** `failureOutcomeEdgesStated` requires state-machines.md to keep "a failed session on a pool with `maxConcurrentSessions: 1`"; DOCS-1's retargeted want-string is a substring of the new row and contains no retired phrase.
- **Part A call counts.** They hold only where the hook is the sole releaser; borrow them as end states on any path with the handler revoke, `failPhase`, or D6.
- **Test rows land with their deliverable.** A test list under a code step must not include rows a later step makes true. The CODE-7 Files-touched line names no test file; do not add one alone.
- **Signature changes outside "every caller in the same step".** CODE-2's `ReclaimClaimed` change leaves `reclaimFinalizedPod` and `createdSweeperReclaim` on the old form until CODE-3 and CODE-4; pass `false` there. `resume.go:295` is a fourth `SlotClaimer` construction the CODE-1 item 4 rule covers (see **Open**).
- **Resume-family test discrimination.** "Released once" passes with and without CODE-5; the old coordinator's binding survives only until its next Sweeper pass or a `ReportAttachStreamFailure`, so hold both off.
- **Seal loss is P2's.** Do not add a seal to D6 or let the stale replica seal.
- **7a CREATE race and the harness.** Keep the winner live or opt into `RelaxOrphanClaims`; keep the checker's `bound` filter.
- **rollbackBinding test pool.** It discriminates only on a `recycle.enabled` pool.
- **Inverted existing tests.** The landing step inverts the named stamp-failure, ready-write, and `credential_lookup` tests rather than treating failures as regressions. `compensation_loss_test.go` keeps its assertions, because it releases each reservation once and reads no slot key (sixth remedy; Retargets names it).
- **Materiality.** Docs-only staleness and Go-comment polish are refuted on materiality: the `shutdown` builder doc, users_invalidate.go:28, the `userTerminateRPCTimeout` comment, pkg/adapter/mcpruntime.go:278, `AfterRevokeWrite`'s "its fan-out sends Shutdown", and tests/tier5_e2e_kind/eager_claim_e2e_test.go comments; an implementor of CODE-4 or CODE-7 may fix them in passing. Departures this proposal creates (CODE-3 items 2 and 3, CODE-5's seal loss, A25) need a record. Register rows A16 to A19 and A21 carry "None" doc sites without a reason, and rows A1, A3, and A24 name DOCS-1 item 10, which states only the claim GC; both are non-spec register cells that a spec round must not file.
- **Single-source items left unfiled.** The F-7.3.34(b) reason at three sites, the F-10.1.21 residue at two, the GC bound clause, and the summary watch-outs; only "A disposition error after the member is removed must not be retried" copies both rule and reason (CODE-1 item 5). The summary **Problem statement.** under-count sentence stays.
- **Plan-conformance traps.** Grep summary, non-spec, and spec staging before filing "member never mentioned"; R21 and P5b's plan entry lacking C3 are plan-side staleness; the claim-register row is never a spec-loop finding.
- **Hard wraps defeat literal greps.** spec/29 is hard-wrapped (about 105 columns; 3-space step and 2-space bullet indents), SPEC-2f and SPEC-2k carry the predicate across a wrap, and CODE-8's comment anchors wrap across comment lines; collapse whitespace before counting. The §4.7 `Shutdown`, §15.1 `created`, §24.11, §28.8 `CH-ATTACH`, and §5.2 `Concurrent` rows are one physical line each.
- **Shell traps.** The glob `*spec-changes.md` matches both files, the non-spec file first; name the file exactly. The ugrep wrapper aliased as `grep` fails on long bounded-repeat patterns; use `/usr/bin/grep -E`. Do not run `pkill -f <script>` in the same Bash command as the script's heredoc, because it kills the invoking shell (exit 144). A per-literal regex scan of tests/ over spec/ takes minutes; pre-filter on 400-character windows.
- **Kubernetes recheck scope.** No staged change adds a non-owner status write, a command inbox, a finalizer, an admission dependency, or a synchronous controller step; by-name delete ABA readings without a concrete duplicate-release path were rejected in rounds 9, 12, 15, 19, and 24.
- **§12.4 "single-key operations only" predates 0093.** §12.4's exception-class sentence "Each of these stores issues single-key operations only" was already false for the slot-set store (the INCR Lua read the `rehydrated` flag), and it is an isolation statement rather than a performance one. Do not file it as a missing edit site or a performance finding (spec.26.review-fresh.1, spec.26.review-performance.1).

### [spec.27.review-reliability.1]
- DECISION: no spec-loop finding under the reliability lens. BECAUSE the spec staging has not changed since b63e249b4 (sixth remedy; `git log` on the spec-changes file), spec/ has no commit since 85c9ab19a, and every crash, re-send, and outage path I traced through SPEC-1a/1b/1c/1d/1f and SPEC-3a/3b lands on an owner decision or a register row. Self-seeding release after the outage-end flag delete: it seeds rows plus its own member, so a duplicate release reaches a second disposition only when a later non-terminal session names the pod, and that session's member keeps the set non-empty (consistent with the spec.25 O19 UNVERIFIED). A go-redis EVALSHA re-send is idempotent for reserve and leaked release, and a clean-release re-send skips the disposition (A9). A handling-replica crash between a cancel commit and step 4(a) is A1/D17 (GC; Counted). A crash after step 1's returns and before the deny write or the cancels is repaired by a repeated full_revoke, which re-lists the still-active rows and any lease minted since. ALTERNATIVES: (1) filing the spec's silence on the Sweeper backstop for a lost step-2 pub/sub release request. Rejected: §11.4's Note already made the step-2 `Shutdown` publish at-most-once before 0093, and SPEC-3b only renames it. (2) Filing the no-TTL flag and the leaked-only set that outlive a terminated pod. Rejected: this predates 0093 (an INCR string never auto-deleted), and a set that empties is deleted by Redis. Memory growth also belongs to the performance lens. (3) Filing the disposition as non-retryable because it runs only on the removing release. Rejected: this is the recorded A1/A9 property with a GC backstop, and the tree's INCR has the same property.
- FACT: spec/ never states the Sweeper's terminal-row binding release (grep "sweeper" finds only REG-COORDMIRROR at spec/28:138 and spec/29:1376). The §11.4 Note's "peer replicas apply them ... within seconds" (spec/11:278) is the only cross-replica release statement, and it predates 0093. EVIDENCE: spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:278
- USEFUL [Standing context Settled: Release-path self-seed; Traps: Release self-seed]: these spared me re-deriving the peer-lock-wait interleavings of SPEC-1a under the merge seed.
