The SPEC-2d projection clause is a staging-consistency question, and spec.27.review-mechanism.1 rejected it as alternative (2). The flag-lifetime tier-11 assertion and the resume.go and resume_rebind.go Targets omission route to the non-spec loop. The `ReleaseRevokedBinding` `ReadPrimary` pin was judged below the bar. The O19 reachability and fallback-admission entries are UNVERIFIED verification questions for a non-spec reviewer. Spec round 27 recorded no new OPEN.

### [non-spec.12.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: the outage ledger is keyed by (pod, session); a later recording replaces the entry's kind, and a leaked failed release sets a sticky leaked mark that the rebuild turns into the addition `leaked:<sessionId>` — BECAUSE the per-session admission drop removed a live same-session re-reservation (the §5.2 slot retry `ReleaseSlotReservation` then `BindSlot` with the same req, slot_bind.go:401-420; the snapshotless resume reaching `bindSlotWithRetry`), discarded an admission on a different pod Q, and the holder filter dropped a leaked failed `/start` release whole (slotbinder.go:230-235 passes `sbe.Leaked`) — ALTERNATIVES: a same-pod or order condition on the drop (an exception clause on a filter); latest-wins without a sticky mark (the slot retry's leaked release followed by a same-pair fallback re-reservation loses `leaked:S`, fail-open); an append-only list (re-introduces "duplicates collapse" and drop rules); a holder-only leaked exception (misses the slot-retry case).
WATCHOUT: `Counter.Release`'s pre-rebuild self-clear drops only the failed-release kind of its entry and keeps the leaked mark; removing the whole entry loses an earlier leak. The post-script removal must skip an entry a recording changed after the rebuild read the ledger, because under keying a recording made during the rebuild lands in the same entry. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 8 **Outage ledger** bullet and the paragraph after `rebuildScript`.
WATCHOUT: `rebuildScript` now takes plain removals then plain additions (`leaked:<sid>` members are passed as additions by Go). Do not reintroduce a per-removal leaked flag in the script: that would be a second encoding of one leak.
WATCHOUT: withdrawn: O17's sentence "The replay removes no live reservation" and item 8's "a new reservation of the same session on the pod cannot precede the replay". Both were false (ClaimSlot Pass 1 joins any same-tenant non-terminal claim with capacity, slotclaimer.go:411-466, so no disposition is needed for a same-session re-reservation on the same pod). Do not re-add either in any wording.
MISTAKE: the Settled entry "Admission drop cannot hit a live re-reservation" was written under the pre-sixth-remedy filter (any non-terminal row is a holder); the sixth remedy's `holds[id] == P` test made it false for a row naming no pod. Deleted this round.
OPEN: cross-replica interleave. Replica A's ledger holds a failed release (P,S) and replica B's ledger holds a later fallback admission (P,S); keying is per `Counter`, so if A's rebuild runs after B's, A's SREM removes S's live member, and S is uncounted until a rehydration finds S's row naming P. No register row bounds this (A26 covers uncounted fallback admissions before a rebuild; A27 covers kept members and must not be widened without the owner). A later round should decide whether A26's bound covers it or the owner needs a new row.
CORRECTS [Settled: Failed-release replay at the recovery rebuild]: removed "admissions of a session that also has a failed release" and "an admission is dropped only when its session's failed release is replayed", and changed "deletes its own failed-release entry" to "drops its own failed release from its entry", to match the keyed ledger.

### [non-spec.12.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: The recording trigger of CODE-1 item 8's **Outage ledger** bullet now names the `rebuildAfterOutage` a `Release` runs first beside its script and its rehydration — BECAUSE a retry during a sustained outage drops its own failed release, then fails inside the rebuild before its script, and the old enumeration left that last attempt unrecorded, so O17's replay never ran — ALTERNATIVES: restating the trigger in the outage-clock bullet (a second rule home); restoring the dropped entry on any failure (also closes the Postgres path below, unreviewed); skipping the caller's own entry inside the rebuild (new parameter, conflicts with single-flight).
WATCHOUT: Do not reorder `Counter.Release` to rebuild before it drops its own failed release: the rebuild then SREMs the member with no disposition and O17's in-bound retry case fails. Do not widen A27 for the rebuild-failure path; the trigger fix closes it. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 8 **Outage ledger** bullet; pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/fallback.go `isRedisUnavailable` returns false for `context.DeadlineExceeded`.
OPEN: A retry that drops its own failed release and then fails in `rebuildAfterOutage` on a SlotSource (Postgres) error records nothing, so it loses the entry an earlier attempt recorded. A27 case (2) covers an entry never recorded, not one recorded and then dropped. Candidate remedy: the attempt restores its dropped failed release when it fails for any reason. A later round should decide whether this is a finding.
DECISION: Tier-1 ledger-ordering cases appended to the CODE-1 tier-1 recovery-rebuild bullet, and one randomized `-race` clause added to TEST-1 Tier 7a — BECAUSE no listed case exercised record-during-rebuild, deadline recording, the sustained-outage retry, or the in-bound clean retry. The record-during-rebuild case uses the replayed pair as well as a fresh pair, because only the same-pair case discriminates the "changed after the rebuild read the ledger" clause under (pod, session) keying. The fake source's `ClassifySessions` is the hook between ledger read and clear, so no production hook is needed. — ALTERNATIVES: new case-table rows (grow the table and register cross-references); tier-1 `-race` for the invariant (test-coverage.md routes ordering to 7a).
WATCHOUT: The in-bound retry case is a clean release with no concurrent rebuild. A leaked release keeps its leaked mark and the rebuild adds `leaked:S` (remaining 1); a joined rebuild that already read the entry turns the outcome into A28.

### [non-spec.12.fix-G3.1]
DECISION: the release of a fallback-admitted session that no rebuild counted is recorded once, as a sentence in case table row O1's Replace-seed cell (Merge-seed inherits it through "As replace-seed"), and register row A28 is re-scoped to "the claim stays `bound` on an empty set because no occupancy-zero disposition runs", with triggers O17 (replay) and O1 (uncounted release). O1's Register row cell now reads "A26, A28". A28's Edge-case site and Pinning test cells name O1 as well. A28's Bound (GC, as for A20), REC-1 action, and Doc site (DOCS-1 item 10, which is generic) are unchanged — BECAUSE A28 already carries this bound, filing, and doc site, and O1 is the one row that states a fallback admission's member state, which spans the O9, O15, and O16 windows — ALTERNATIVES: adding the clause to A26 was rejected because DOCS-1 item 9 names A26's outcomes and would owe a reader sentence, and A26 would get two backstops; editing O16 alone covers one window only; a new A29 row would break every "A26 to A28" range; disposing on `remaining == 0` when `removed` is false would break exactly-once disposition (A9, D8); keeping a terminal admission in the rebuild would be an A27-type Counted leak, and A27 must not be widened.
WATCHOUT: the O1 sentence's qualifier "while the pod's `rehydrated` flag is set" is what makes it true. With the flag absent, `Release` self-seeds the session through `Counter.rehydrate`'s extra member, the `SREM` removes it, and the disposition runs. A pod first claimed during the outage has no flag, so the finding's own example (a create that claimed P during the outage) disposes normally. A test of this case must arrange a flag that was set before the outage, and must arrange that no listed pod's rebuild has deleted it — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 1 `releaseScript` (`EXISTS` → `{-2,0}`), item 2 `Release` signature bullet (self-seed), item 8 `rebuildScript` (DEL flag only when ARGV[1]=='1').
FACT: the tree's unkeyed `releaseScript` is `DECR` (pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go:228), and `fallbackReserve` (fallback.go:98) writes no counter state. Today a release after a fallback admission therefore reaches zero and disposes, so 0093 introduces this residue — EVIDENCE: slotcounter.go:204,228.
CORRECTS [spec.25.review-reliability.1]: the Open entry is verified and closed. The residue also occurs on the admitting replica, because its rebuild drops a terminal admission. It is now named by register row A28 (case table row O1), so I deleted the Open entry from Standing context.
WATCHOUT: the lifecycle harness checker's invariant "no pod with an empty set and no non-terminal row keeps a `bound` claim" (non-spec-changes.md **Harness extension**) is false under both A28 triggers. It holds today only because no harness scenario injects a Redis outage. A round that adds an outage scenario to the harness must give it an A28 relaxation — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md Harness extension bullet, TEST-1.

### [non-spec.12.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: the outage ledger keeps one entry per (pod, session); a later recording replaces the entry's kind (admission or failed release), and a leaked failed release sets a leaked mark no later recording clears; the rebuild drops a removal whose session holds a slot on P (`holds[id] == P`) and an admission whose row is terminal, and turns every leaked mark into the addition `leaked:<sessionId>`. The per-session admission drop is deleted. `rebuildScript` loses its per-removal leaked flag (removals list, then additions list). O5 and F2 become pointers to CODE-1 item 8 and O17 — BECAUSE the entry then records the net effect the session's own scripts would have had (reserveScript adds S; releaseScript removes S and adds `leaked:S` whether or not SREM removed one), which closes all three G1 findings with one model — ALTERNATIVES: (1) a per-session drop with a same-pod or ordering condition (rejected: order-dependent exception clause, hair); (2) replaying a holder's leaked failed release in full (rejected alone: equivalent after the flag-delete reseed, but a later admission of the same pair would still erase the leak under latest-wins, so the sticky mark is needed anyway); (3) the reviewer's latest-wins without a sticky leaked mark (rejected: the §5.2 slot retry's leaked `ReleaseSlotReservation` followed by a fallback re-reservation of the same pair would drop `leaked:S`, fail open).
FACT: two tree paths re-reserve the same session on the same concurrent pod during one outage: the §5.2 slot retry (`ReleaseSlotReservation` then `BindSlot` with the same req) and `bindConcurrentSlot`'s `bindSlotWithRetry` branch for a row with no pod or in recovery. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/slot_bind.go:42-55, :406-421
FACT: `releaseScript`'s leaked arm adds `leaked:<sid>` whether or not SREM removed a member, so a sticky leaked mark in the ledger matches the non-outage script. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 1 releaseScript block ("The leaked arm adds its member whether or not `SREM` removed one")
OPEN: cross-replica residue. A failed release (P,S) in replica A's ledger and a fallback re-admission (P,S) in replica B's ledger are not merged; if A's rebuild reads S's row before the commit names P and runs after B's script, S is live with no member. The deleted per-session drop never covered this either (ledgers are per Counter). Not in A26 or A27's four cases; whether a resume of S can be driven by a second replica within one outage is unverified. A later round should file it and, per the Standing context, it needs its own register row and an owner decision (A27 must not be widened without asking).
CORRECTS [review-log Settled **Admission drop cannot hit a live re-reservation**]: its premise (a resume's row is non-terminal, hence a holder) died with the sixth remedy's slot-holder filter, and the rule it defends is deleted by this design; the §5.2 slot retry re-reserves the same ID without a resume.
CORRECTS [review-log Settled **Failed-release replay at the recovery rebuild**]: "admissions of a session that also has a failed release" and "an admission is dropped only when its session's failed release is replayed" no longer hold; the ledger is keyed (pod, session) with latest-wins and a sticky leaked mark.
WATCHOUT: `Counter.Release`'s pre-rebuild self-delete must drop only the removal from its (pod, session) entry and keep the leaked mark, or a terminal release retry after an earlier leaked release of the same pair loses `leaked:S`. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 8 **Outage ledger** bullet

### [non-spec.12.fix-design-G3.1]

DECISION: the skipped disposition when a fallback-admitted session ends before it is counted goes into register row A28 (re-scoped to "claim stays `bound` on an empty set because no occupancy-zero disposition runs", two triggers: O17's replay and O1's uncounted release) and one sentence in case table row O1's Replace-seed cell (inherited by Merge-seed through "As replace-seed"), with O1's register column gaining A28 — BECAUSE the residue has A28's exact bound (GC, as A20), REC-1 action (File, as A11), and doc site (DOCS-1 item 10, which is generic), and O1 is the one row that states a fallback admission's member state across the O9, O15, and O16 windows — ALTERNATIVES: the finding's A26 clause (rejected: A26's doc site is DOCS-1 item 9, whose "outcomes that rows ... assign" would then owe a new reader sentence, and A26 would carry two backstops); a new row A29 (rejected: cascades into REC-1 item 5, A11's REC-1 cell, summary Decision 21, and CODE-1 item 8's residue list, all of which say "A26 to A28"); `ReleaseSlot` disposing on `remaining == 0` without `removed` (rejected: breaks the exactly-once disposition that A9 duplicates, D8 withdrawals, and peer-lock waits rely on); the admitting replica treating its own ledger admission as removed (rejected: new mechanism that misses the O9 non-observing replica); keeping a terminal admission in the rebuild (rejected: adds a member no release removes, an A27-type Counted leak, and A27 may not widen without the owner).
FACT: the residue needs the pod's `rehydrated` flag set at the release. With the flag absent `releaseScript` returns {-2,0}, `Release` rehydrates with the session as an extra seed member, the SREM then removes it, and the disposition runs when nothing else remains. On the admitting replica the rebuild deletes the flag only for pods `ListPodsWithActiveSlots` lists, so when the session was the pod's last the flag keeps whatever value it had (set on a pod used before the outage, absent on one first claimed during it). EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 1 `releaseScript` and item 2 `Release` signature bullet; CODE-1 item 8 `rebuildScript` `ARGV[1]`.
FACT: the tree's unkeyed release clamps at zero and returns the count, so a zero there drove the disposition even for a fallback-admitted session; 0093's keyed `removed` gate introduces this residue. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go `Counter.Release` doc comment and body (around :460-476); pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/fallback.go `fallbackReserve` writes no member.
WATCHOUT: A28's sentence "The owner decided the replay" is provenance for the O17 case only; the O1 case is a filed departure with A20's bound and needs no owner acceptance. Do not read the caller's "A28 holds the empty-set replay" as a closed set the way A27's four cases are. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md register row A28; review-log Standing context sixth-remedy entry item (3).
CORRECTS [spec.25.review-reliability.1]: verified. The release removes nothing and skips the disposition on a non-observing replica in the O9 window and also on the admitting replica (its rebuild drops the terminal admission before the release's script), but only while the pod's `rehydrated` flag is set; the residue is now register row A28's O1 case, so the Open entry is closed.

DECISION: the cross-replica replay (replica A's failed release of S on P replayed after replica B re-reserved S on P before S's row names P) is staged as a third case of register row A26 (O17 sentence, A26 description, bound "second and third cases have no bound in time", edge site O17, DOCS-1 item 9 clause, a two-`Counter` tier-1 pin), and raised as the summary's one open decision — BECAUSE no design close exists without an owner: the only filter that closes it (replay only when the row is terminal or names another pod) keeps the member of every failed funnel, suspension, and resume-compensation release, which widens A27 (owner-accepted as exactly four cases) — ALTERNATIVES: a new row A29 (more citer edits: A11's REC-1 cell, REC-1 item 5, CODE-1 item 8's residue list, summary Decision 21, DOCS-1 item 9); a `resuming`-only skip (does not close it).

CORRECTS [Settled: Failed-release replay at the recovery rebuild]: the script description is now "SREM the kept failed releases, then SADD the kept admissions and a `leaked:<sid>` for every leaked mark", keyed per (pod, session); "A13 and A26 are unchanged by the replay" was false after this round and now names A26's new case.
CORRECTS [Standing context: Open decisions for the human]: no longer "None"; the summary carries the A26/A27 replay decision.
CORRECTS [non-spec.12.fix-G1.1 OPEN cross-replica interleave] and [non-spec.12.fix-G2.1 OPEN source-error self-clear]: both closed as above.

### [non-spec.12.review-mechanism.1]

CORRECTS [Settled: Admission drop cannot hit a live re-reservation]: that reasoning held only while the replay filter dropped a failed release whose row is non-terminal. The sixth remedy's slot-holder filter replays a failed release whose row is `resume_pending` or `resuming` (pod cleared), so the per-SESSION admission drop ("an admission whose session also has a failed release that the rebuild replays", non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 8) now drops a resume's live fallback admission, on the same pod or another one. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:255, :267, :190 (O17 "The replay removes no live reservation").
FACT: a concurrent-pool snapshotless resume reserves through `ClaimSlot` Pass 1, which joins any same-tenant non-terminal claim with capacity, so a resume can land on the pod whose failed release is in the ledger; O17's "no disposition returns the pod for the session's resume to claim" covers only the checkpoint branch (`claimPod`, idle pods). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_rebind.go:60-68; pod_launch.go:177-181; slot_bind.go:42-52, :380-421; podsession/slotbinder.go:658-672; podclaim/slotclaimer.go:411-466.
FACT: resume attempts run during a Redis outage, because the coordination lease store fails over to Postgres. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/redis_quota.go:209-211.
DECISION: proposed fix keys the outage ledger by (pod, session) with the latest entry winning, and deletes the per-session admission drop — BECAUSE it subsumes the drop's legitimate purpose (admission then failed release on one pod) and makes O5's existing "adds the ledger's admitted sessions whose rows are not terminal" correct, so it is a reduction — ALTERNATIVES: scoping the drop to the same pod (still wrong when the session re-reserves the same pod after the failed release, which Pass 1 allows); a new register row (adds an unbounded fail-open residue the owner has not accepted).
WATCHOUT: single-flight lets a `Counter.Release` retry join a rebuild that already read its failed-release entry; that rebuild replays it, so the retry removes nothing and runs no disposition. The consequence is A28 (GC), so it was not filed, but O17's "removes the member with its own script, and runs the disposition" holds only without a concurrent rebuild. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:190, :253, :267.
UNVERIFIED (performance lens): while the outage clock is set, every `Reserve` and `Release` runs `rebuildAfterOutage` before its script, so during the outage itself each slot call (and each `releaseSlotRetrying` attempt) pays `ListPodsWithActiveSlots` plus `ClassifySessions` before the script fails; single-flight shares only concurrent calls. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:255, :268.

### [non-spec.12.review-reliability.1]

- FACT: The same session identifier can be re-reserved on the same concurrent pod through `ClaimSlot` Pass 1, which excludes no pod: the §5.2 slot retry policy re-calls `BindSlot` with the same `req` after `ReleaseSlotReservation` (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/slot_bind.go:377-420), and the snapshotless resume-rebuild branch reaches `bindSlotWithRetry` on a recovery row (resume_rebind.go:59-71 -> pod_launch.go:177-181 -> slot_bind.go:42-55). Before the commit or `persistPodAssignment` the row names no pod, so `holds[S] != P`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go:411-432
- CORRECTS [Settled: Admission drop cannot hit a live re-reservation]: that entry says a same-ID re-reservation happens only on resume and is safe because the row is non-terminal. Since the sixth remedy the replay filter is holder-based (the row names P), not non-terminal, and a resuming or slot-retry row names no pod. A rebuild between a fallback re-admission and the row naming P therefore replays the failed release and drops the admission, which leaves the live session uncounted (non-spec-changes.md:255, :267, O17 at :190).
- WATCHOUT: the rebuild's holder filter drops a leaked failed release whole. BindReservedSlot releases with `sbe.Leaked` (podsession/slotbinder.go:230-240), so a failed `/start` with an unclean cleanup during an outage loses its `leaked:` member. The no-outage path keeps it (row D6b; tier-1 "clean then leaked" row). EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:190, :255
- WATCHOUT: the ledger recording trigger (non-spec-changes.md:253) enumerates "its script or its rehydration", while a retry deletes its entry before `rebuildAfterOutage`, and the rebuild fails first during a sustained outage (:268). Read strictly, every attempt after the first loses the entry.
- FACT: The Open UNVERIFIED "Fallback admission released on a non-observing replica" is real, and it is broader than stated. It also occurs on the admitting replica: its rebuild runs before the release's own script, drops the admission because the row is terminal, and the script then removes nothing and skips the disposition. The claim is left `bound` for the GC. No register row records it; A28 covers only a replay that empties a set. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:255, :188-189, :562, :564
- USEFUL [Settled: Sixth remedy]: its list of recovery-state writers saved a re-grep. MarkBudgetUnrecoverable lives at pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationbudget/adapters.go, not pkg/gateway/delegationbudget/.

### [non-spec.12.review-test-coverage.1]
DECISION: filed one test-coverage finding: CODE-1 item 8's outage-ledger ordering rules have no named test. The rules are an entry recorded while a rebuild runs keeps the clock set and survives to the next rebuild, a release cut by its context deadline while the clock is set records an entry, and a retry inside F2's bound deletes its own entry before the rebuild. BECAUSE the tier-1 rebuild bullet (non-spec-changes.md:434) covers "one case for each row" plus four named extras. O5 (:178) states only the after-clear re-stamp, and no tier-7a case touches the ledger (:497). A naive implementation passes every listed test. ALTERNATIVES: (1) filing the tier-4/tier-8 "Redis keeps its data" outage cases as needing a missing test-infra pause. Rejected: tests/testinfra/containers/redis.go has only Stop (Terminate), but `DEBUG SLEEP` or `CLIENT PAUSE` through the container, or chaos.PartitionService (toxiproxy), can produce the outage without new infra. (2) A gateway wiring test for `RunRecovery`. Rejected: storagequota's RecoveryReconciler has no wiring test either (cmd/lenny-gateway/reconciler_workers.go:202), so the repo norm does not require one. (3) A28's slotcounter pin as vacuous. Rejected: the replay is structurally disposition-free.
FACT: Go's socket read-deadline error (internal/poll DeadlineExceededError) does not satisfy errors.Is(err, context.DeadlineExceeded). Only net's dial errTimeout does (/usr/local/go/src/net/net.go:613-634). So `isRedisUnavailable` (pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/fallback.go:150-176) classifies a go-redis read timeout as unavailable, and it treats only a context-deadline expiry as the caller's.
FACT: The existing tier-4 and tier-8 slot outage tests inject the outage with containers.Redis.Stop, which terminates the container and loses the data. The tier-8 "recovery" case starts a second container and a second Counter. EVIDENCE: tests/testinfra/containers/redis.go:94-101; tests/tier8_chaos/slot_counter_redis_outage_test.go:125-160.
WATCHOUT: the TEST-1 harness checker requires each concurrent pod's set to equal exactly the non-terminal rows naming the pod plus the `leaked:` members (non-spec-changes.md:485). The predicate paragraph (:166) says a failed `/start` stays `ready`, keeps naming the pod, and has no member. Any harness scenario that ends with a failed `/start` still `ready`, or a tier-8 departure pin (A3, A4) that does not opt into the slot relaxation, will trip the checker. Listed scenarios currently end such sessions terminal. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:166, :485, :508-509

### [remedy.terminal-member-sweep.2026-10-18]
DECISION: owner decision C of 2026-10-18 applied. The recovery rebuild no longer replays failed releases. It adds each replica's add-only ledger entries (fallback admissions and leaked marks) to each pod the listing or the ledger names, reads those pods' members, classifies the session members with one `TerminalSessions` read, and removes every member whose session's row is terminal, keeping every `leaked:` member; a listed pod's flag is deleted in the same script. The ledger and the sweep are stated once, in the **Outage ledger and terminal-member sweep.** paragraph beside the case table; CODE-1 item 8, F2, the Counted backstop, A4, A26 to A30, DOCS-1 item 9, the tests, summary Decision 21, the reseed Non-goal, the watch-outs, and the spec-changes edge bullet cite or restate it from that paragraph — BECAUSE a terminal session cannot transition further (`Terminal` in pkg/session/state/state.go), so a removal keyed by a terminal identifier repeats that session's own release and is safe from any replica, it covers an exited replica's failed terminal releases, and it never removes a live re-reservation, which option A did — ALTERNATIVES: (A) the per-replica failed-release replay with per-(pod, session) keying, a sticky leaked mark, and a drop-and-restore retry protocol, rejected by the owner because one replica's replay removes another replica's live re-reservation of the same session (fail open, no time bound) and because every round-12 finding was a defect in its ordering protocol; (B) the same replay limited to sessions whose row is terminal or names another pod, rejected by the owner because it keeps the ledger and protocol and leaves an exited replica's failed releases counted; (C') deleting the flag of every swept pod rather than only listed ones, rejected because it contradicts SPEC-1b's staged flag-lifetime sentence ("for each pod a non-terminal session names") and would reopen the spec loop, so a sweep on a ledger-only pod is an A28 trigger instead.
DECISION: the first script adds the ledger's entries and returns the members, and the sweep classifies after that add — BECAUSE classifying admissions before the add (the replay design's admission drop) left the window A27 case (4) recorded, in which another replica releases a fallback-admitted session between the read and the script; with add-then-read, a terminal write and release before the add is seen by the read, and one after the read finds the member — ALTERNATIVES: re-classifying the added admissions after the script (a second read for the same result).
DECISION: `leaked:` members whose session is terminal are kept — BECAUSE §6.2 **`leaked` slot semantics.** says a leaked slot persists until pod termination and counts toward the unhealthy threshold, and nothing in the spec frees a leaked slot at its session's end — ALTERNATIVES: removing them with the session (fails open on the leaked cleanup's resources and drops the §6.2 threshold count).
DECISION: A27 is now the one owner-chosen clause, "non-terminal, non-holder members stay counted" (rows O15, O16, and O18); its four earlier cases are retired: case (1), an exited releasing replica, is closed by the sweep for every swept pod; cases (2) and (3), a release with no ledger entry, no longer depend on the ledger, and the member is swept when its row is terminal or stays under A4's own Counted bound when no rebuild runs; case (4), the read-to-script window, is closed by add-then-read except when the release left the row non-terminal, which is the owner's clause. A26's third case (the replay removing a re-reservation) is deleted with the replay.
DECISION: two consequences of C that the owner's decision did not name are filed as new register rows and put to the owner as the summary's one open decision rather than folded into A27 — A29: a terminal member on a pod no rebuild sweeps (the pod's last session ended during the outage, so the listing omits it, or no observing replica survives), Counted and GC-bounded unless a same-tenant session joins first through `ClaimSlot` Pass 1 (pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go Pass 1 joins any non-terminal same-tenant claim with capacity); A30: the sweep removes a terminal session's member before its own release has shut the runtime down (a pending hook or D6 release, a release no replica performs under A1 to A3, A24, and A25, or a leaked mark lost with an exited replica), so the pod can admit one over its cap until that release runs, with no time bound when none runs; a Redis restart's reseed already drops the same members — BECAUSE the summary watch-out requires a new residue to get its own row and an owner decision, and the owner said not to widen A27.
CORRECTS [non-spec.12.follow-up-fix.1 DECISION and the summary open decision **A replay that removes another replica's re-reservation**]: closed; the owner chose C, and the sweep keeps a non-terminal session's member whichever replica reserved it.
CORRECTS [non-spec.12.fix-G1.1], [non-spec.12.fix-design-G1.1], [non-spec.12.fix-G2.1], [non-spec.12.fix-design-G2.1], and [non-spec.12.follow-up-fix.1 second DECISION]: the per-(pod, session) keying, the sticky leaked mark, the self-clear and restore protocol, and the recording trigger for failed releases are deleted with the replay. The leaked-mark recording trigger (script, rehydration, or the rebuild it runs first, or the deadline while the clock is set) survives for leaked marks only.
FACT: the round-12 ledger is complete: state-non-spec.json records round 12 with every lens retired except review-mechanism, review-reliability, and review-test-coverage, and each of those, the three fix pairs, and the follow-up fix has an entry above. No missing non-spec.12 entry was written — EVIDENCE: scratchpad/cp-state/0093-p1/state-non-spec.json.
WATCHOUT: the sweep's test is the session row's terminality, never the **Slot-holder predicate**. A holder-based removal removes a live re-reservation of a session whose row names no pod yet. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md **Outage ledger and terminal-member sweep.**, rows O14 and O18.
WATCHOUT: the flag delete stays scoped to listed pods because SPEC-1b's staged sentence scopes it; a sweep on a ledger-only pod therefore loses a pending release's disposition (A28). EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-1b fifth substitution; non-spec-changes.md register row A28.
FACT: every staged anchor of SPEC-1a to SPEC-3b (55 strings, fenced and inline) matches the spec tree once with whitespace collapsed, checked by script on 2026-10-18 after this remedy; the two "When occupancy reaches zero on a recycling pod" anchors match once each in their own targets (SPEC-2b in spec/04, SPEC-2h in spec/06), and spec/ has no commit since 85c9ab19a. This remedy edited no staged spec edit, only the edge-case bullet now named **The recovery rebuild's terminal-member sweep**.

### [remedy.terminal-member-sweep.2026-10-18.review]
DECISION: the independent review of a21907f99 found one sweep defect: a leaked release of a terminal session that failed with no leaked mark (a Postgres error or `ErrRehydrationStalled` in its rehydration, or a deadline before the clock was stamped) left the plain member, which a later sweep removed, so the leaked slot stopped being counted (fail open, against §6.2 **`leaked` slot semantics.**). Fixed by recording a leaked mark on every failure of a leaked `Release`, whatever the error and whether or not the clock is set; recording stamps the clock, so a rebuild adds `leaked:S` — BECAUSE the mark is add-only, so over-recording is safe, and it closes all three paths without a new register case — ALTERNATIVES: naming the three paths in A30, the O17 cell, A4, and the open decision (adds a fail-open residue the owner has not seen when a safe close exists).
CORRECTS [remedy.terminal-member-sweep.2026-10-18, second CORRECTS]: the leaked-mark recording trigger is no longer limited to Redis-unreachable failures and the deadline while the clock is set; every failure of a leaked `Release` records a mark.
CORRECTS [Settled: Merge equals replace after a restart]: the empty-set case is a sweep (A28), no longer a replay (A27). [Traps: Round-26 declined re-files]: `ClassifySessions` renamed `TerminalSessions`. [Settled: Staged anchors]: a third edge bullet changed.
FACT: the review found no false tree claim, no other path that removes a live member, owner decisions 16, 21, and 22 preserved, A27 exactly the owner's clause, and no change outside the proposal directory or to staged spec edit text.

### [remedy.add-only-rebuild.2026-10-19]
DECISION: owner decision of 2026-10-19 applied ("I want something simple that will just work and in fail closed cases the GC will clean up afterwards"). The outage-end recovery rebuild is add-only. It reads the ledger, lists the pods non-terminal per-slot rows name, and runs one `rebuildScript` per pod the ledger or the listing names, which adds the ledger's admissions and leaked marks and deletes the `rehydrated` flag of a listed pod, as SPEC-1b's staged flag-lifetime sentence requires. It removes no member. Deleted: `rebuildSweepScript`, `TerminalSessions` (store method, `pgstore` and `memstore` implementations, and its tier-4 case), the add-before-read ordering, the sweep test cases, and register rows A29 and A30. Kept from option C: no per-replica failed-release replay and no failed-release ledger entries. The leaked marks stay, because a fallback-admitted session holds no member for its failed leaked release to keep, and a later clean release of the same session would otherwise remove the only trace of the leak. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md **Outage ledger and add-only rebuild.**, CODE-1 item 8, register rows A27 and A28.
DECISION: register row A27 restated as the owner's accepted clause, "a member whose release failed during an outage stays counted until a later release of it or the orphan GC after the pod's last live session", covering terminal and non-terminal sessions on a live or an exited replica (case table rows O15 to O18). The owner accepted it on 2026-10-19. A29 (a terminal member on a pod no rebuild covered) is the same case and is merged into A27; A30 (a sweep removal before the session's own release) no longer occurs and is deleted; A28 keeps only row O1's uncounted release, because its second case was the sweep on a ledger-only pod. A26 had no sweep-caused case.
DECISION: rejected alternatives, recorded in summary Decision 21. (1) Option C's terminal-member sweep: it removed a terminal session's member before that session's own release had shut its runtime down, and the member of a session whose leaked mark an exited replica held, so the pod admitted over its cap with no bound in time short of the release or the pod's termination; that is a fail-open window. (2) The same sweep limited to terminal writes older than one coordination Sweeper listing plus the per-slot cleanup floor (20 seconds by default): the age is an arbitrary timing heuristic, and it leaves the lost-mark and unperformed-release cases open.
DECISION: the summary's open decision (residues A29 and A30) is closed, the owner having answered it. No staged spec edit states a rebuild removal, so no staged edit text or anchor changed; only the spec-changes edge bullet was renamed to **A member whose release failed during a Redis outage stays counted** and pointed at A27.
DECISION: new test obligations: the tier-7a invariant that no rebuild ever removes a member; a tier-4 case in which the session that ended during the outage on a replica that then stopped stays counted after the rebuild, the pod's last release leaves the claim `bound`, and `GetActiveSlotsByPod` returns no session for the pod (the read `PodHasActiveSession` makes); and a tier-8 case in `orphan_gc_recovery_test.go` in which the GC drains a pod that holds such a member within `claimOrphanTimeout` plus one sweep interval.
CORRECTS [remedy.terminal-member-sweep.2026-10-18 and its review]: the sweep, its ordering rule, the A28 sweep case, and rows A29 and A30 are withdrawn; the leaked-mark recording trigger (every failure of a leaked `Release`) stands.

### [remedy.add-only-rebuild.2026-10-19.review]
DECISION: the independent review of 59548b902 found five errors, all fixed. (1) The tier-4 case asserted through `sessionActiveLookup.PodHasActiveSession`, an unexported type in `cmd/lenny-controller` that a tier-4 test cannot call and the compose stack does not run; it now asserts `GetActiveSlotsByPod` returns no session, the read that lookup makes. (2) A27 had absorbed the re-add of a fallback-admitted session whose release ran on another replica before the admitting replica's rebuild; that release did not fail, so A27 went beyond the owner's clause. The case is now its own row A31 (same Counted-then-GC bound, rows O15 and O16, DOCS-1 item 9 sentence), and A27 is exactly the owner's clause. (3) A28 claimed an empty set after the uncounted release, but the admitting replica's rebuild re-adds the member (A31); A28 is now limited to the admitting replica exiting before its rebuild (rows O1 and O11). (4) O15's merge-seed cell said the earlier release "removed nothing", which is false when another replica's rebuild deleted the flag and the release self-seeded; it now says that release cannot remove the member the script adds later. (5) The reseed Non-goal cited A27 for every missed release; it now cites the Counted and GC backstops and rows A1 to A4 and A27.
CORRECTS [remedy.add-only-rebuild.2026-10-19, second DECISION]: A27 covers rows O17 and O18 only; rows O15 and O16 are A31; A28 is rows O1 and O11.

### [spec.28.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no finding in spec round 28 under the performance and failure-mode lens — BECAUSE the only change to the spec staging since b63e249b4 is the A27 edge bullet (59548b902, renamed **A member whose release failed during a Redis outage stays counted**), no staged edit text or anchor moved, and `git log 85c9ab19a..HEAD -- spec/` is empty, so the round 23 to 27 rate math stands (one reserve plus one release script per session, about 400 Lua calls/s at 200 sessions/s against the ~6,500/s §12.4 Tier 3 budget; SPEC-2 adds no etcd write) — ALTERNATIVES: (1) filing the post-outage capacity loss of the add-only rebuild at Tier 3. The add-only rebuild is an owner decision (2026-10-19), and the residue is register row A27, so its remedy is outside the spec staging. (2) The outage ledger's memory. Rejected: admissions are bounded by one 60 s fallback window, about 12,000 entries per replica at 200/s.
FACT: shipped `Counter.Release` runs no fallback and no rehydration. During a Redis outage it returns the error and leaves the INCR count raised. A failed-release member that stays counted therefore matches the shipped code. It falls short only of the shipped spec's "writes the accurate count" rehydration, which no code implemented. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go:461-476

### [spec.28.review-reliability.1]

FACT: Since a21907f99 the spec staging changed in one place only: the A27 edge bullet, renamed **A member whose release failed during a Redis outage stays counted**. No staged edit or anchor moved, so a reliability recheck of SPEC-1a to SPEC-3b needs only that bullet plus the add-only paragraph it cites. EVIDENCE: `git diff a21907f99 HEAD -- <proposal>.spec-changes.md`
DECISION: no reliability finding against the spec staging in round 28. BECAUSE every recovery path I traced through the staged spec text either works or is an accepted, register-bounded departure. The traced paths: a lost-reply or duplicate keyed release (SPEC-1a's removed-and-empty gate, A9, A13), a crash between the member removal and the claim disposition (A9 with the GC backstop), a leaked release on a pod whose last session leaks (no disposition, GC or the slot-health drain, as before), the outage-end flag delete racing a release or a peer's rehydration (merge keeps members, the blocked release retries after the flag is set; A26 on a replica that did not observe the outage), a full_revoke crash between the step-1 lease returns and the step-2 cancels (removing the record rejects the token; a repeat cancels), and a lost step-2 pub/sub release (the holder's Sweeper). ALTERNATIVES: filing the session-keyed release's inability to tell two bindings of one session apart, when a bounded counter-retry lands after a resume re-reserved the same pod. I rejected it as speculative: CODE-5 and D6 fence stale bindings by generation, and any remedy would be non-spec.
USEFUL [Standing context Traps: Release self-seed; Round-26 declined re-files; Redis restart semantics]: these saved re-deriving three near-findings: the peer-seeded release that removes nothing, the per-replica flag-delete burst, and an AOF restart that keeps the flag.

### [spec.28.review-security.1]
DECISION: no security finding on the staged spec edits in round 28 — BECAUSE the only spec-changes diff since b63e249b4 is the A27 edge bullet (renamed **A member whose release failed during a Redis outage stays counted**), spec/ has no commit since 85c9ab19a, and the bullet's "removes no member ... which fails closed" matches the add-only paragraph and register row A27 (non-spec-changes.md:566): a kept member only lowers admission. Re-checked: the slot set stays gateway-owned with the §12.4 Postgres fallback kept by SPEC-1d (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:219), no bound is sourced from a pod self-report, SPEC-2c's mcs-1 failed-or-crashed delete matches §5.2's "regardless of recycle settings" (spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:531), and SPEC-3a returns and deny-lists leases before the cancel writes, which tightens full_revoke — ALTERNATIVES: (1) filing A27 as a reuse relaxation on a non-recycling concurrent pool (a stale member keeps the claim `bound`, so a later same-tenant session can join through Pass 1 without the occupancy-zero drain, and `maxSessionsPerPod` is unset when `recycle.enabled: false`); rejected because the join stays tenant-pinned, a continuously loaded Bounded-cohort pod already admits joiners the same way (spec/05:428), and Traps **Pass 1 joins any live tenant claim** and Settled **A27 does not relax the retirement bound** cover it. (2) Filing A28's skipped occupancy-zero disposition or A26's unbounded uncounted admission as missing spec edge bullets; rejected because both are owner-accepted register rows, Traps **Edge bullets stay pointers** bars filing a bullet's omission, and A26 against SPEC-1d is a declined re-file.
FACT: `recycle.maxSessionsPerPod` is required only when `recycle.enabled: true` (spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:551), so the Session-count-limit backstop the A27 Settled entry cites does not exist on a Bounded-cohort pool (`maxConcurrentSessions` N, `recycle.enabled: false`); there the A27 member is bounded only by the GC or a later release. EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:428,524,551
USEFUL [spec.27.review-security.1]: its RBAC, admission, and §5.2 retire-sentence checks still hold against an unchanged spec/; I re-verified only the changed bullet.

### [non-spec.13.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: `ListPodsWithActiveSlots` filters with the predicate of the 0080 partial index `idx_sessions_active_by_pod` (`pod_assignment <> ''` plus the terminal-state clause), stated in CODE-1 item 8's first bullet — BECAUSE the Slot-holder predicate names no pod for '', and item 9's clear writes '' on every `resume_pending` and `awaiting_client_action` edge, so a listing that returns '' makes `rebuildScript` run with KEYS `lenny:pod:{}:active_slots` and `lenny:pod:{}:rehydrated`, which hash to different Cluster slots (CROSSSLOT), O7 keeps the clock set, and the rebuild never completes — ALTERNATIVES: a Go skip of '' in `rebuildAfterOutage` (second statement of the filter; memstore and fakes would still return ''); citing only the Slot-holder predicate (an implementor copying `GetActiveSlotsByPod`'s SQL still lists '', because that method's empty guard is Go rather than SQL).
WATCHOUT: `GetActiveSlotsByPod`'s empty-pod exclusion is a Go guard (`if podID == ""`), and its SQL carries only the terminal-state clause. Do not describe a new query as "the predicate `GetActiveSlotsByPod` uses" when the empty-string exclusion matters; name the 0080 index predicate. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go `GetActiveSlotsByPod`; migrations/0080_sessions_active_by_pod_index.up.sql (`WHERE pod_assignment <> '' AND state NOT IN (...)`).
FACT: go-redis `hashtag.Key` treats `{}` as no hash tag (`e > 0` requires a non-empty tag), so a key with an empty pod id hashes whole and two such keys land in different slots. EVIDENCE: ~/go/pkg/mod/github.com/redis/go-redis/v9@v9.19.0/internal/hashtag/hashtag.go `Key`.
DECISION: the Tier 4 `pgstore` assertion "returns no empty string" sits on the funnel-edged `resume_pending` row sentence — BECAUSE it is the only Tier 4 fixture holding an empty `pod_assignment`; on the terminal/exclusive-pool sentence the assertion would pass vacuously.

### [non-spec.13.review-mechanism.1]
DECISION: two bookkeeping findings and no mechanism finding on the add-only rebuild — BECAUSE every flow traced through the **Outage ledger and add-only rebuild.** paragraph, rows O1 to O19, CODE-1 items 7 to 9, F1/F2/F8/F11, and register rows A26 to A28 and A31 holds: leaked-mark retry ordering (rebuild SADDs `leaked:S` and keeps S, then the retry's script SREMs S and re-adds `leaked:S`), fallback-admission release on the admitting replica (rebuild before script), a reserveSlot/slot-retry rollback of a fallback admission that fails during the outage (A27 via O18's header), and the F8 committed-then-error edge write (F11's guarded clear misses, release proceeds) — ALTERNATIVES: (1) filing the leaked-mark clock stamp on a non-Redis error (Postgres, ErrRehydrationStalled, deadline) as an unlisted case-table trigger that causes a cluster-wide flag delete with no outage; rejected: the paragraph in the same section states it, O3 states the cost, and A13 already covers the duplicate exposure. (2) Filing the per-call `ListPodsWithActiveSlots` during an outage (non-spec.12.review-mechanism.1 UNVERIFIED); rejected as cost, not a broken mechanism.

### [non-spec.13.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: filed one finding: CODE-1 item 8 defines `ListPodsWithActiveSlots` as the distinct `pod_assignment` values of non-terminal per-slot rows with only the terminal-state predicate, which returns '' (every recovery-state row after CODE-1 item 9, and podless suspended rows). The rebuild then runs `rebuildScript` for pod '', whose keys `lenny:pod:{}:active_slots` and `lenny:pod:{}:rehydrated` hash to different Redis Cluster slots (an empty tag hashes the whole key), so on the Cluster provider every rebuild fails with CROSSSLOT and, per row O7, the clock never clears. Fix: `pod_assignment <> ''` in the listing (the 0080 partial index already carries it) plus a tier-4 assertion — BECAUSE the paragraph says the rebuild lists "the pods the Slot-holder predicate names", and '' names no pod — ALTERNATIVES: a rebuild-side skip of an empty pod (kept as an optional second guard, since a leaked `Release` on pod '' would also record a mark).
FACT: go-redis v9.19 `hashtag.Key` requires at least one character between the braces (`e > 0`); `{}` hashes the whole key. CRC16 slots: `lenny:pod:{}:active_slots` 9273, `lenny:pod:{}:rehydrated` 13354 — EVIDENCE: ~/go/pkg/mod/github.com/redis/go-redis/v9@v9.19.0/internal/hashtag/hashtag.go:50-57
FACT: the 0080 partial index is `WHERE pod_assignment <> '' AND state NOT IN (...)`; a listing query without `pod_assignment <> ''` neither excludes '' nor matches that index — EVIDENCE: migrations/0080_sessions_active_by_pod_index.up.sql:22-25; pgstore.go:836-852 guards '' only in `GetActiveSlotsByPod`.
WATCHOUT: the outage clock now does two jobs: it marks the §12.4 fallback window start and it marks that a rebuild is needed. Leaked-mark recordings stamp it for non-Redis errors (a Postgres error, a deadline), and it stays set until a rebuild succeeds with an empty ledger. That makes the window run from the first stamp rather than from Redis becoming unreachable. I judged this below the bar: it can only fail closed early, and only under overlapping Postgres and Redis trouble. Do not re-file it as a fail-open — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:172-173, :270; fallback.go:123-133.
WATCHOUT: rebuilds run single-flight, and the probe's rebuild runs on `w.watchdogCtx` with no deadline. A hung Postgres listing would hold every joiner until TCP keepalive gives up. I did not file this. The storage-quota and delegation reconcilers follow the same pattern without deadlines, and code-best-practices.md already requires a deadline on outbound calls. An implementor should still bound the rebuild and let a joiner leave when its own context ends — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/quota/storagequota/recovery.go:110-130.
USEFUL [Settled: Recovery rebuild placement and scope; Traps: Round-26 declined re-files]: these saved me re-deriving the per-replica flag-delete cost and the fail-closed `Reserve` under a Postgres failover.

### [spec.29.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no finding in spec round 29 under the performance and failure-mode lens. BECAUSE the only spec-changes diff since 59548b902 (round 28's text) is the citation in the edge bullet **A reseed drops leaked members and unpersisted reservations** (bbc747ec9: "rows O13 and O14" became "**Outage ledger and add-only rebuild.**"), no staged edit or anchor moved, and `git log 85c9ab19a..HEAD -- spec/` is empty. The round 23 to 28 rate math therefore still holds: one reserve and one release script per session (about 400 Lua calls/s at 200 sessions/s against the ~6,500/s §12.4 Tier 3 budget), and SPEC-2 adds no etcd write, because the gateway's release delete replaces the controller's termination delete and SPEC-2d removes a status patch. ALTERNATIVES: (1) Filing SPEC-1b's outage-end flag delete "for each pod a non-terminal session names" as Redis DEL amplification over exclusive pods, which carry no flag. Rejected: it is at most one DEL per pod per outage per replica (about 10^4 pipelined DELs at Tier 3), deleting an absent key costs nothing durable, and CODE-1 filters to `best-effort-per-slot`. (2) Filing a Sentinel failover that loses an acknowledged SADD as a fail-open regression. Rejected: the INCR counter lost the same write, and the CODE-1 clock observes the failover as an outage, so the flag delete plus the merge seed re-adds the member when its row names the pod. That is no worse than the shipped behaviour. (3) Filing the rehydrating-lock TTL (2000 ms) expiring under post-outage Postgres load, which lets two seeds run at once. Rejected: both seeds only add members, and the stale-seed race predates 0093 (Traps **Pre-existing findings to leave unfiled (spec.25)**).
FACT: §5.2's burst paragraph says `GetActiveSlotsByPod` "returns at most `maxConcurrentSessions` rows". That still holds under SPEC-1b's identifier seed while Decision 22's slot-holder predicate stands, because a non-terminal row naming the pod is a slot holder. EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:593
USEFUL [spec.28.review-performance.1] and [Traps: Round-26 declined re-files]: they scoped this round to a single-hunk diff and saved re-deriving the A27 capacity-stranding argument and the per-replica flag-delete repeat.

### [spec.29.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no reliability finding on the staged spec edits in round 29 — BECAUSE `## Staged edits` is unchanged since round 26 and spec/ has no commit since 85c9ab19a; re-traced SPEC-1a (idempotent keyed reservation and release, release-path self-seed, leaked arm), SPEC-1b (merge seed, flag lifetime), SPEC-1f, and SPEC-3a/3b (return-then-deny, cancel then release, step 4 reclaim, bounded local releases) through restart, outage end, lost replies, and replica exit; every residue lands on a register row (A4, A9, A11 to A13, A26 to A28, A31) or a settled trap — ALTERNATIVES: (1) a second leaked release of one session on one pod collapsing two leaks into one `leaked:<sid>` member; rejected because a session cannot re-reserve on a pod that still holds its leaked member: resume uses `Claimer.Claim` (idle or reserved-hold pods only, pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resume.go:183, claimconnect.go:44), and a pod with a `leaked:` member never reaches occupancy zero, so only the already-filed /start-retry-without-reservation Defect reaches it. (2) SPEC-1b's "deletes it ... when a Redis outage ends" versus a leaked mark recorded on a Postgres error or deadline stamping the clock and so deleting flags with no Redis outage; rejected as benign (the merge seed only adds) and its remedy would be non-spec (narrow the stamp), not a spec edit.
FACT: step 4(a)'s reclaim is bounded: `terminalReclaimPreRunning` calls `ReclaimClaimed`, whose delete runs on `detachedReleaseContext` = `context.WithTimeout(context.WithoutCancel(ctx), slotCleanupFloor)`, so SPEC-3b's "each local release is bounded" is not falsified by the reclaim — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/reclaim.go:99-100; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/usage.go:527
WATCHOUT: under CODE-1 item 8 a failed rebuild makes `Reserve` return `ErrFailClosed` on a source failure, and a leaked mark recorded on a Postgres error stamps the clock, so a Postgres-only failure during a leaked release turns every `Reserve` on that replica into a rebuild attempt until Postgres answers. This is a non-spec concern (session create needs Postgres anyway); do not file it in the spec loop — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 8 clock bullet; **Outage ledger and add-only rebuild.** first two bullets
USEFUL [Traps: Pre-existing findings to leave unfiled (spec.25)]: closed the rehydrating-lock stale-seed race and the outage-onset lost-reply member without re-derivation.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: Only `fallbackReserve`, an unreachable probe tick, and a `Counter.Release` that fails because Redis is unreachable stamp the outage clock; the rebuild runs while the clock is set or the ledger holds an entry, and the listing and the flag delete run only while the clock is set (bullets 2 and 3 of **Outage ledger and add-only rebuild.**) — BECAUSE the listing and flag delete exist only to count fallback admissions, which only Redis unavailability produces; stamping on a deadline, Postgres error, or `ErrRehydrationStalled` leaked mark made one per-pod failure delete every concurrent pod's `rehydrated` flag, made `Reserve` fail closed through a Postgres-only failover, contradicted SPEC-1b's "when a Redis outage ends", and started the §12.4 fallback window early (fallback.go `outageExceeded` measures from `outageSince`) — ALTERNATIVES: a second "saw Redis unreachable" bit beside the clock (duplicates the clock's meaning, keeps the early-window side effect); recording leaked marks only on Redis failures (loses the leak, fails open); inline retry of the leaked release (item 5's `releaseSlotRetrying` already retries); no ledger-only rebuild on the probe tick (an idle replica would hold the mark unapplied).
WATCHOUT: A zero clock with a nonempty ledger is now a legal state (a non-Redis leaked mark awaiting a ledger-only rebuild). The tier-7a invariant is the event form "never clear the clock while the outage ledger holds an entry"; do not restore the state form "never leave a nonempty ledger with the clock cleared". EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md TEST-1 **Tier 7a (`-race`).**
WATCHOUT: The leaked-mark recording rule (bullet 1, "whatever the error ... whether or not the outage clock is set") is unchanged and must stay; only the stamp rule narrowed. Row O3 and CODE-1 item 8's clock and `RunRecovery` bullets now cite bullet 2 as the one home of the stamp and trigger rule; do not re-enumerate the stamping events there. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md **Outage ledger and add-only rebuild.**, row O3, CODE-1 item 8.
FACT: In the tree `isRedisUnavailable` treats a context deadline as the caller's signal and not a Redis outage, and `outageExceeded` lazily stamps `outageSince`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/fallback.go `isRedisUnavailable`, `outageExceeded`.
DECISION: The summary watch-out "The recovery rebuild removes no member." lost its rejected-removal sentence, and the Non-goal on terminal-member sweeps keeps one clause pointing at Decision 21 — BECAUSE Decision 21 already points at the review log's **Outage rebuild: rejected alternatives** — ALTERNATIVES: a pointer sentence in the watch-out (a second pointer two bullets below Decision 21's).
OPEN: The same watch-out's leaked-mark sentence ("The ledger keeps recording leaked marks, because ...") restates bullet 1's reason; left by design for a later round.
DECISION: DOCS-1 item 9's A26 sentence now states that a session admitted during the outage can stay uncounted when the admitting replica restarts before it rebuilds, and that while uncounted the pod can admit over the limit and the session can lose its pod — BECAUSE A26's Bound assigns the pod-failure outcome and item 9 is A26's only doc site; "the replica that admitted it restarts before it rebuilds" covers both O11 disjuncts — ALTERNATIVES: "one session too many" (several fallback admissions can exceed the cap by more than one).

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: Only a Redis-unreachable failure stamps the outage clock (`fallbackReserve`, an unreachable probe tick, a `Counter.Release` that fails because Redis is unreachable). A leaked mark recorded for any other failure (context deadline, Postgres error or `ErrRehydrationStalled` in rehydration) stamps nothing. The rebuild runs while the clock is set OR the ledger holds an entry, and it lists pods and deletes flags only while the clock is set; otherwise it runs the ledger's per-pod scripts alone (ARGV[1] '0'). BECAUSE the listing and flag delete exist only to count fallback admissions, which only Redis unavailability produces; stamping on a non-Redis failure made one stalled leaked release delete every concurrent pod's flag, made `Reserve` fail closed during a Postgres-only failover with Redis healthy, and contradicted SPEC-1b's "when a Redis outage ends". It uses the clock itself as the "saw Redis unreachable" bit, so it adds no state. ALTERNATIVES: the finding's separate "found Redis unreachable since last rebuild" bit (a second state that duplicates the clock); stop recording non-Redis leaked marks (that loses the leak when a later clean release removes the session's member, which is the reason bullet 1 records on every error).
WATCHOUT: this gate is not the withdrawn "gating on 'this replica admitted'" (standing context, Recovery rebuild placement and scope). It gates on the replica having observed Redis unreachable, which is the O8/O9 replica-view split the table already uses. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md rows O8, O9.
WATCHOUT: the probe tick must also run the ledger-only rebuild. Without it, an idle replica holds a non-Redis leaked mark unapplied while other replicas admit over the cap; the old design applied it within one probe interval because the mark stamped the clock. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 8 RunRecovery bullet.
FACT: `outageExceeded` stamps `outageSince` lazily and measures the §12.4 fallback window from it, so stamping the clock on a non-Redis failure also shortened the next real outage's fallback window. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/fallback.go:128-132. `isRedisUnavailable` treats a context cancel or deadline as "the caller's signal, not a Redis outage". EVIDENCE: fallback.go:155-157.
WATCHOUT: after the redesign the tier-7a state invariant "never leave a nonempty outage ledger with the clock cleared" is false (zero clock plus a non-Redis leaked mark is legal). It becomes the event invariant "never clear the clock while the ledger holds an entry". EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md tier 7a bullet.
DECISION: O3's Case cell cites **Outage ledger and add-only rebuild.** for what stamps the clock rather than enumerating it, so the paragraph's bullet 2 is the one home of the stamp rule. CODE-1 item 8's clock bullet and RunRecovery bullet point at it too. BECAUSE four sites stated the stamp rule and two had already drifted.
DECISION: summary watch-out "The recovery rebuild removes no member." loses its second sentence outright (Decision 21 already points at the log's rejected-alternatives entry); the Non-goal keeps a one-clause reason. ALTERNATIVES: the finding's replacement pointer sentence (a third pointer to the same log entry, two bullets below Decision 21's).
OPEN: the watch-out's remaining sentence "The ledger keeps recording leaked marks, because a session the fallback admitted holds no member ..." restates bullet 1's reason ("It counts the leak of a session that holds no member"). It was a copy before this round, so it is not obsolete here; a later single-source round may cut it.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G3.1]
DECISION: Decision 15's reason clause is replaced to name the adopter (current-binding holder) as the releaser through its own funnel, `releaseEarlierBinding`, or D6, and the stale replica releases nothing; the CODE-5 item 2 eviction list is unchanged — BECAUSE after a takeover, `MarkBudgetUnrecoverable` writes `awaiting_client_action` with no release and no generation change, so the adopter's binding stays current and its D6 (terminal) or `releaseEarlierBinding` (resume on the adopter) releases the pod; eviction on the stale replica stays correct — ALTERNATIVES: narrowing the eviction list to exclude MBU-reachable states (wrong: a release from the stale replica would hit the adopter's pod at a stale generation); the reviewer's wording naming only funnel and D6 (omits `releaseEarlierBinding`, which releases the MBU row when the adopter resumes it).

UNVERIFIED: whether a resume driver on replica B can take a `resume_pending` row that `MarkBudgetUnrecoverable` left naming a pod bound on replica A with NO prior takeover (A's lease lapsed or never renewed outside running). If yes, B's `releaseEarlierBinding` finds no local binding, B's `resuming` write makes A's binding stale with a resume-family fromState, CODE-5 evicts on A, and nobody releases A's pod, which also falsifies Decision 15's first clause ("exists only on a replica that lost a running-state takeover"). Who: a mechanism-lens round; check coordination lease renewal for non-running rows (acquireCoordinationLease, resume_driver.go:146) and the watchdog's resume trigger.
OPEN: R11's binding column ("None, because the failure funnel releases the binding ...") omits the `MarkBudgetUnrecoverable` row, whose binding stays current on the holding replica; full_revoke step 4(b) behavior for that case is unstated. Separate finding, not fixed by G3. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md R11 row, Slot-holder predicate paragraph first bullet.

### [non-spec.1.review-kubernetes.1]
DECISION: no non-spec finding under the Kubernetes-idiom lens. BECAUSE the non-spec staging adds no SandboxClaim or Sandbox status writer, no field manager, no finalizer, no webhook dependency, no CRD used as a message bus, and no controller step on a request path. The new loop (CODE-1 item 8 `Counter.RunRecovery`) is a per-replica gateway goroutine on `w.watchdogCtx`, which is a plain `context.WithCancel(context.Background())` and is not leader-gated, so O2, O4, and A26's per-replica probe bound hold. CODE-7's peer release uses pub/sub, not a CRD. ALTERNATIVES: (1) The GC-versus-Pass-1-join window on an A27 pod: the claim stays `bound` and joinable, and the GC can drain it between a joiner's Redis reserve and its row INSERT. Rejected: the joiner fails closed, and the same GC race exists today for any concurrent pod whose only counted slot is an in-flight reservation (A16 family). (2) The tier-4 A27 case asserts "leaves the claim `bound`" on a topology of bare `Counter`s with no kube-apiserver. Rejected: an implementor can wrap the `Counter`s in a `SlotClaimer` with a controller-runtime fake client, and nothing in the staging forbids that.
FACT: The orphan GC is a leader-elected `manager.Runnable`. It reads `PodHasActiveSession` through the controller's own Postgres pool (`cmd/lenny-controller/stores.go:54`). It drains, then deletes, a `bound` or `recycling` claim once `BindingStateTransitionTime` (falling back to creationTimestamp) plus `claimOrphanTimeout` has passed. The defaults are 5 minutes and a 60-second interval. This matches the register's **GC** backstop and DOCS-1 item 10's `--claim-orphan-timeout`. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/gc.go:51, :134-139, :233-241, :276-290; cmd/lenny-controller/flags.go:177
FACT: The tree has no WarmPoolController claim delete at pod termination. The only controller deletes of a SandboxClaim are the GC's drain-then-delete and its precondition-guarded reserved DELETE. A `released` or `failed` claim is never deleted, and the Sandbox finalizer ignores terminal claims (`activeClaimReferences`). The disposition table's **Paths outside the table** sentence ("the WarmPoolController's deletes at pod termination and orphan GC") and §4.6.3 row 619 both describe a termination delete that does not exist in the tree. This gap predates 0093 and nothing in 0093 depends on it, so it was not filed. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/gc.go:309-312, :363, :390; pkg/controller/sandbox/finalizer.go:60-76, :109-122; non-spec-changes.md:68
WATCHOUT: The Sandbox session-cleanup finalizer holds deletion while any non-terminal claim references the pod. So on a pod with an A27 or A28 residue, a Sandbox delete stays Terminating until the GC deletes the `bound` claim. This is GC-bounded, and the bound is the same as every other GC-backed register row. It is not a new stuck-finalizer footgun. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/sandbox/finalizer.go:64-75
FACT: CODE-1 item 9's `pod_assignment` clear on the funnel's recovery edges does not affect the §10.1 orphan-session reconciler, because its eligible states exclude `resume_pending`, `resuming`, and `awaiting_client_action`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/orphansession/orphansession.go:68-74
USEFUL [Traps: Kubernetes recheck scope]: this entry still holds for the full non-spec staging after the add-only rebuild and the prune. It cut the sweep to the GC backstop, the CODE-8 regeneration, and the new probe loop.

### [non-spec.1.review-mechanism.1]

FACT: `MarkBudgetUnrecoverable` lives at pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationbudget/adapters.go:137 (not pkg/gateway/delegationbudget); it moves a root to `awaiting_client_action` with no release and no `coordination_generation` bump. So on a replica that lost a running-state takeover, a terminal write with `fromState` `awaiting_client_action` (or `resume_pending` after `enterResumePending`) can meet a stale binding whose pod the adopter still holds; the adopter's D6 releases it, not its funnel. CODE-5's evict outcome stays right; summary Decision 15's reason does not cover this path. — EVIDENCE: summary.md:31; non-spec-changes.md:164; adapters.go:137-150
FACT: Outage-clock stamping triggers are stated with different conjuncts: the ledger paragraph stamps on any recorded entry, including a leaked `Release` that fails on a Postgres error, `ErrRehydrationStalled`, or its context deadline while Redis answers, but case-table row O3 names only Redis-unreachable failures. Each stamp costs a full listing and a flag delete on every listed pod. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:172-173, :185
FACT: The tier-4 Redis container helper has only `Stop` (terminate), with no pause (tests/testinfra/containers/redis.go:94). TEST-1's tier-4 outage case says "paused with its data kept", so the implementor needs a pause primitive (docker pause or `CLIENT PAUSE`). I judged this below the bar.
WATCHOUT: A vacuous rebuild (empty ledger, empty listing) runs no script and "succeeds", so it clears the clock while Redis is still down. I judged this harmless: the window start equals the first admission, and the probe re-stamps within one interval. Do not re-derive it as a fallback-window defect. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:177; pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/fallback.go:120-131
USEFUL [Settled: Slot-holder predicate needs no spec edit]: the readers I checked (orphansession eligible states, coordination.PodHeld, checkpointer freshness, classifyCommit, expireRaisedElicitations keyed by session) do not read a recovery-state row's pod, so item 9's clear is safe for them.

### [non-spec.1.review-performance.1]

FACT: The outage clock is one per `Counter`, so one per gateway process; the add-only rebuild lists every pod the Slot-holder predicate names (Postgres, one cross-tenant `SELECT DISTINCT` over the 0080 partial index, which does not filter `scrub_policy`), and then runs one script per pod. While the clock is set, every `Reserve` and `Release` on the replica runs that whole rebuild first and fails if any part of it fails (non-spec CODE-1 item 8, and the tier-1 case "a rebuild whose source fails ... makes `Release` return the source error"). — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:174, :177, :267, :433
FACT: Tier-3 sizing for this lens: 10,000 active sessions (spec/17_deployment-topology.md:1114), 5 to 30 gateway replicas (spec/17:964), Redis Cluster required at Tier 3 (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:270), §5.2's example of 500 concurrent pods (spec/05:593), and about 400 slot Lua calls/s (Standing context **Slot-set cost.**). — EVIDENCE: as cited
FACT: Shipped `Reserve` calls `clearOutage` on any Redis answer, and a Redis failure diverts only the call that hit it (`slotcounter.go:280-290`). `isRedisUnavailable` treats a context deadline as not unavailable (`fallback.go:155-157`). — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go:277-311, fallback.go:150-176
WATCHOUT: Under Redis Cluster, CODE-1 item 7's hash tag spreads pods across shards, so an outage of one shard is a partial outage. The process-global clock and the all-pods rebuild turn that partial outage into a replica-wide one: every pod's `Reserve` falls back (and fails closed 60 s after the first stamp), and every pod's `Release` fails, which leaves A27 members on pods whose shard never failed. Filed as a finding. Any fix has to keep the rebuild-before-fast-path guarantee for each pod (row O5), and may scope it per pod. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:254, :267, :189 (O7)
WATCHOUT: A leaked mark that is recorded for a non-Redis failure (ErrRehydrationStalled, a Postgres error, or a context deadline) still stamps the clock. The replica then deletes the `rehydrated` flag of every listed pod, which no SPEC-1b event allows, and it couples every slot call to Postgres until a rebuild succeeds. Filed as a finding. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:172-174; spec-changes.md:94
WATCHOUT: The rebuild lists pods (Postgres) before it touches Redis. During a Redis outage, each `Reserve` or `Release` attempt on a stamped replica therefore runs one fleet-wide scan before it fails, including every attempt of the F2 retry loop and every call made after the fallback window has closed. Filed as a finding. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:174, :267
OPEN: A27 magnitude at Tier 3. Each clean release that fails during an outage leaves one member. At about 200 releases/s fleet-wide, a 60 s outage leaves about 12,000 members, which exceeds `maxConcurrentSessions` on most of about 500 to 2,500 pods. Those pods then admit nothing until their last live session ends, and the GC frees them about 6 minutes later. The shipped tree has the same stale counts (its DECR fails and no rebuild runs), so no regression is filed. DOCS-1 item 9 and A27's "admits one session fewer" describe one member, and an owner may want the aggregate stated.

### [non-spec.1.review-reliability.1]

- FACT: The session server's lifetime context is cancelled by `Server.Close`, which `runServer` calls only after `httpSrv.Shutdown(ctx)` has drained in-flight HTTP handlers and before `gatewayCtrlSrv.GracefulStop`. So CODE-6's lock wait on `s.lifetime()` does not skip releases for HTTP-handled terminal writes during the drain. Only the short window between `Close` and process exit can hit F6's "Server shutdown" cell (A1). EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/runserver.go:213-226; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/sessionserver.go:1865-1868
- FACT: `w.watchdogCtx` is a plain `context.WithCancel(context.Background())` and is not leader-gated, so CODE-1 item 8's `RunRecovery` probe runs on every replica, as rows O2, O4, and O9 assume. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/controlserver.go:273-278; cmd/lenny-gateway/reconciler_workers.go:202-203
- FACT: The tree has exactly one `slotcounter.New` call, so one `Counter` (one ledger and one clock) exists per gateway process, and the per-`Counter` ledger and single-flight rules hold per replica. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/pod_lifecycle.go:245
- FACT: `ReleaseTerminalBinding` runs its body in its own goroutine. A D6 release that blocks for the bounded retry of CODE-1 item 5 during a Redis outage therefore does not stall the coordination Sweeper's listing loop. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_binding.go:42-53; pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:530-535
- FACT: `MarkTerminated` raises `coordination_generation` only when the source state is `resuming`. No binding is published in `resuming` on the current coordinator, because `releaseEarlierBinding` releases before the `resuming` write, so CODE-5's eviction never strikes a live binding there. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/terminal_mark.go:37-48; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_rebind.go:168-178
- WATCHOUT: Register row A4's doc-site reason says a release fails "only while Redis fails or, when it finds the `rehydrated` flag absent, while the Postgres read of its rehydration fails". Since CODE-1 item 8, a release also fails, with the flag present, whenever the outage clock is set and the rebuild's `ListPodsWithActiveSlots` fails. A leaked mark recorded on a non-Redis error can set that clock. I judged this below the bar: terminal writes need Postgres too, the conclusion of the cell holds, and the effect fails closed. Do not re-derive it as a new departure. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md register row A4; **Outage ledger and add-only rebuild.** bullets 1 and 2
- UNVERIFIED: CODE-1 item 8 does not say whose context a single-flight rebuild runs on, or whether a waiter honours its own deadline. A rebuild started by the probe runs on `w.watchdogCtx`, which has no deadline. An implementation in which waiters block on that shared rebuild would stall every slot reservation and release on the replica while the rebuild's Postgres listing hangs. Check this at implementation time, because the staging leaves it to the implementor; it was not filed.

### [non-spec.1.review-security.1]

FACT: The outage clock cannot be extended past one fallback window by the add-only rebuild. `clearOutage` runs only after a rebuild that leaves the ledger empty, and the first `fallbackReserve` admission records a ledger entry that only a successful Redis script removes. A rebuild with an empty ledger and an empty listing runs no script and clears the clock without contacting Redis, but no admission has happened at that point, so nothing is admitted fail-open. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/fallback.go:122-145 (`outageExceeded` stamps only when zero); non-spec-changes.md:172-177 (ledger and clear rule).
FACT: In the tree, a store error in `terminateUserSessions` still runs `runFullRevokeFanOut`, including token revocation, for the rows cancelled so far. CODE-7 keeps this as step 4(c) (failure-outcome row F14), so token revocation is not lost on the error path. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/users.go:563-571; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/users_invalidate.go:145-195.
FACT: `credassign.Client.Release` sends the `RevokeCredentials` RPC before `leases.Remove`, so each lease's token stays valid for up to one RPC timeout during step 2. In the tree, the deny write ran only after the pod-terminate fan-out, so this is not a regression. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credassign/client.go:363-377; users_invalidate.go:151-160.
FACT: `w.watchdogCtx` is a process-lifetime context with no leader election (cmd/lenny-gateway/controlserver.go:273), so the CODE-1 item 8 recovery probe runs on every replica, as O2 and A26 assume.
WATCHOUT: `MarkBudgetUnrecoverable` lives at pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationbudget/adapters.go:137, and the proposal's `delegationbudget/adapters.go` is a suffix of that path. It also moves `created` and `ready` roots, which hold no binding, so `releaseEarlierBinding`'s clear does not reach them. The row keeps naming the old pod until the resume commit, and the residue fails closed through the GC. I judged this below the bar.
USEFUL [Standing context: Traps "Step-1 crash and latency", "Credential facts"]: these settled the Token Service latency and record-removal questions without a re-derivation.

### [non-spec.1.review-test-coverage.1]

DECISION: filed one test gap: the `(true, nil)` success result of the new `ReleaseRevokedBinding` is never asserted against the real function, so nothing pins `podsTerminated` end to end.
