### [non-spec.2.review-performance.1]

FACT: After the round-1 fix, the outage clock (which is also the §12.4 fallback-window start) is stamped only by `fallbackReserve`, an unreachable probe tick, and a `Release` that fails because Redis is unreachable. A leaked mark for a Postgres error, `ErrRehydrationStalled`, or a context deadline leaves the clock zero, and the rebuild it triggers runs the ledger's scripts only, with no `ListPodsWithActiveSlots` call and no flag delete. So a Postgres failover with Redis healthy no longer causes a fleet-wide listing or `ErrFailClosed` on `Reserve`. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:173-174 (Outage ledger bullets 2 and 3); test non-spec-changes.md:433 ("calls no `ListPodsWithActiveSlots`, and deletes no flag ... a `Reserve` on another pod while the fake source fails succeeds on the fast path").
FACT: `isRedisUnavailable` returns false for `context.DeadlineExceeded` and true for "connection pool timeout" and "i/o timeout". A release cut by its own deadline therefore never stamps the clock, and a client-pool timeout does stamp it, which triggers a full rebuild (row O3's per-failure cost, register A13). EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/fallback.go:150-189.
DECISION: I filed no finding on the round-1 diff — BECAUSE the clock/ledger split, the CODE-1 item 8 citation-only bullets, rows O2, O3, and O5, and the changed tier-1 and tier-7a cases are consistent with one another and add no per-call write load beyond one ledger script per call while a ledger entry exists. Under a Postgres failover the failure mode is no worse than shipped, because the shipped leaked release skips Redis entirely. — ALTERNATIVES: I considered and rejected (a) a feedback loop in which a stalled re-recorded leaked mark keeps the clock set and repeats the full listing rebuild, because it is speculative and was not introduced this round, and (b) a pool-timeout-triggered full rebuild at the top tier, which is a variant of the O3 cost already accepted under A13 and of refuted findings.

### [non-spec.4.review-mechanism.1]

Lens: end-to-end mechanism, round 4 of the non-spec loop. No findings filed.

FACT: The existing tier-4 and tier-8 outage tests stay valid under the clock that only a successful rebuild clears. A `Reserve` with the clock set runs the rebuild first. During the outage the rebuild fails on its first script, and `Reserve` then takes `fallbackReserve`, whose `outageExceeded` still measures from the first stamp. The racer subtests (one admission under the advisory lock) and the 61-second fail-closed subtests therefore keep their assertions. The tier-8 recovery half builds a fresh `Counter` with a zero clock. FACT: `waitForFlag` returns nil when its timeout passes and `Reserve` retries the script, so a rebuild's flag `DEL` that lands after a peer's seed costs one more rehydration attempt within `maxReserveAttempts`. It does not strand a waiter. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go:415-436
FACT: `rediskeys.ValidateKey` matches only the `lenny:pod:` prefix, so the hash-tagged keys of CODE-1 item 7 pass it. tests/tier11_docs/redis_key_prefix_registry_test.go pins only spec text and no Go key spelling. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/rediskeys/rediskeys.go:118-143; tests/tier11_docs/redis_key_prefix_registry_test.go:54
FACT: Every writer of `resume_pending`, `resuming`, or `awaiting_client_action` other than the funnel's two mutators and `MarkBudgetUnrecoverable` starts from a row that names no pod. The watchdog's exits run from `resuming`, which names no pod. `enterResumePending` from `suspended` is guarded on `!holdsPod`. The **Slot-holder predicate** bullet holds. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:892-968; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go:535-551
DECISION: Did not file the narrow race in which a ledger-only rebuild (started with the clock zero) clears a clock that `fallbackReserve` stamped mid-rebuild, before the admission is recorded — BECAUSE the next unreachable probe tick or fallback call stamps the clock again within about 2 seconds, and the admission entry keeps the ledger non-empty, so the rebuild still runs. The window measurement moves by milliseconds, and nothing fails open — ALTERNATIVES: requiring the clear only when the clock was set at rebuild start (that is hardening, below the bar).
DECISION: Did not file A4's Doc-site reason ("the release fails only while Redis fails or ... while the Postgres read of its rehydration fails") — BECAUSE a release can also fail when the clock is set and the rebuild's `ListPodsWithActiveSlots` fails, but that is still a Postgres outage covered by the same runbook, so the "None" doc-site conclusion holds. This is wording only. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md A4 row; CODE-1 item 8 rebuild-failure bullet.

### [owner.stale-slot-reaper.2026-10-19]
DECISION: owner decision of 2026-10-19 applied: a leader-run stale-slot reaper releases a plain session member whose session row has been terminal for longer than `claimOrphanTimeout`, through an unconditional adapter `Shutdown` at the pod address and then a keyed removal, keeps the member on any failure, never removes a `leaked:` member, and is rate-limited by an operator-tunable flag. Rule site: the non-spec **Stale-slot reaper** section; failure outcomes: row F21; code: CODE-9; spec: SPEC-1a's added sentences and SPEC-1k; summary Decision 23 and the Decision 7 amendment; register rows A27, A31 (restated bounds), A32, and A33 (new); the **Counted** backstop restated. Owner guidance applied the same day: simplest fail-closed mechanism workable at Tier 3, no timing heuristic beyond `claimOrphanTimeout`, the reaper as the primary capacity recovery because sessions last an hour or more, and a default rate that clears about 12,000 members in a few minutes. BECAUSE the A27 residue at Tier 3 is about 12,000 members after a 60-second outage, and the GC bound waits for each pod's last live session. ALTERNATIVES: running the occupancy-zero disposition on the reaper's removal (rejected: it needs the pool's recycle setting and cleanup commands for the recycle `Shutdown`, and a pod whose only member is stale has no live session, so the GC drains it, register row A32); taking `slotAccountLocks` (rejected: the lock is per replica and the late release runs on another replica; the keyed removal's idempotence serializes them); a reaper over every pod's Redis keys (rejected: a `SCAN` across a Redis Cluster; `ListPodsWithActiveSlots` names every pod with a live session, and a pod without one is the GC's).
DECISION: the release needs no binding. `connectSlot` already reaches a pod's adapter from the Sandbox's `status.podIP` (pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/slotbinder.go `connectSlot`), and the unconditional `Shutdown` is idempotent: with no registry entry it answers `absent` (pkg/adapter/session.go `shutdownReclaimOutcome`, rule 11). EVIDENCE: slotbinder.go:658-735; session.go:279-337 (the non-removing returns at :307 and :335).
DECISION: one addition, SPEC-1k and CODE-9 item 1: an `absent` answer reports an unclean exit while the identifier is under the §5.2 reclaim hold (`s.reclaiming`). BECAUSE today `answerShutdown` reports a clean exit on every non-removing arm (session.go:307 and :335), while a teardown that did not complete keeps the identifier in `s.reclaiming` for the pod's life (`reclaimSlotLocked`, `openReclaimHoldLocked` in pkg/adapter/bindattempt.go:159; `completed` in session.go `tearDownReclaimedSlot`, where an unclean exit implies an incomplete cleanup). Without the addition, an unclean teardown whose leaked release failed during the outage and whose leaked mark was lost with its exited replica leaves a plain member that the reaper's `Shutdown` would answer clean, freeing a slot whose runtime may still run, the lost-mark case that rejected option C. ALTERNATIVES: keeping every member the adapter answers `absent` for (rejected: that is the common case, a release whose `Shutdown` succeeded and whose counter call failed, so the reaper would recover nothing); scoping the unclean answer to a hold whose cleanup has ended (rejected: a reaper racing a late release's in-flight teardown would then remove the member while the teardown can still fail; the in-flight duplicate's consequence, register row A33, fails closed).
FACT: `terminated_at` is stamped once by the first terminal write and kept on a relabel to `failed` (`sessionstore.MarkTerminated`, pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/terminal_mark.go:37-44); the column is from migration 0168 and is NULL on a non-terminal row. `sessions.id` is a `UUID` (migrations/0001_initial_schema.up.sql:75), so non-UUID members are dropped before the query. EVIDENCE: as cited.
FACT: the controller's `--claim-orphan-timeout` defaults to 5 minutes (cmd/lenny-controller/flags.go:177), and the chart does not set it, so the gateway's `--slot-reaper-terminal-age` takes the same default as its own flag. The gateway-singleton sweeps run under `gatewayleader.NewGate` in cmd/lenny-gateway/leader_sweeps.go, and `golang.org/x/time` is already in go.mod. EVIDENCE: as cited.
FACT: the problem statement lists "a releaser for a dead slot on a pod with live siblings (S8)" among the spec gaps; the reaper is that releaser for a member whose session is terminal. EVIDENCE: problem-statement.md:30.
WATCHOUT: the recovery bound is `claimOrphanTimeout` after the session ended, plus up to one pass interval, plus the drain at the rate limit (about 10 minutes for 12,000 members at the defaults). A member of a session whose row is non-terminal (row O18) is released only after that session ends, which the owner's "rare corner cases" allowance covers.
CORRECTS [non-spec.1.review-performance.1 OPEN "A27 magnitude at Tier 3"]: closed by the reaper; DOCS-1 item 9 now states the aggregate recovery time.

### [owner.stale-slot-reaper.2026-10-19.review]
DECISION: the independent review of e89383168 found five errors, all fixed. (1) Rows A32 and A33 had been inserted into the outage case table after row O15 instead of the register; they now follow A31. (2) SPEC-1k keyed the unclean `absent` answer on the whole reclaim hold, which made §5.2 disposition-table rows false: the row for a running slot whose close succeeds and whose other act fails (clean flag "Set", `leaked` "Not entered", hold kept for the pod's life; spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md table row "The runtime close succeeds and any other act fails") and the row for a release outside a `Shutdown` ("Not entered, because nothing carries the outcome to the gateway"). The answer is now keyed on two adapter records: a `Shutdown` teardown of the session in flight, or the last `Shutdown` teardown's own unclean answer; a release outside a `Shutdown` sets neither, and the §5.2 hold-paragraph substitution was withdrawn. The unclean-answer case still implies an incomplete cleanup (`tearDownReclaimedSlot`: an unclean exit implies `completed` false). (3) A33 now names the overlap case and the duplicate after an unclean answer, and its bound names the unhealthy-slot drain threshold. (4) The **Eligible member.** rationale claimed S names no live or future slot; it now states the assumption that no bind of S is in flight `claimOrphanTimeout` after the terminal write, and register row A34 records the bind that outlives the window (fail-open for that start's remaining duration, until `commitStart` refuses and its compensation tears the slot down). ### [spec.30.review-fresh.1]
DECISION: no fresh-read finding in spec round 30 — BECAUSE the only staged-spec text changed since round 29 is SPEC-1a's reaper sentences, SPEC-1k, Design item 1, and the A27 edge bullet; the three SPEC-1k anchors match once each (spec/04 rule 11 and rule 15 lines, spec/07:25), every link target resolves (§12.5 heading at spec/12:282 holds the `lenny-gateway-leader` Lease at :358; **Orphaned `SandboxClaim` detection:** at spec/04:515), and the reaper composes with the unedited sites: §4.7.1 rule 12 ("what every caller other than a compensating reclaim sends"), the §5.2 hold paragraph ("`Shutdown` is the one request outside the hold"), §6.2 **`leaked` slot semantics.** (the reaper never removes `leaked:`), and §4.6.1 predicate 1 (the reaper never empties a set, so the GC still owns a pod with only stale members) — ALTERNATIVES: (1) filing SPEC-1a's reaper keep-on-unclean against SPEC-1a's own set definition ("one member `leaked:<sessionId>` per slot whose cleanup was not acknowledged clean") and §6.2's "equivalently the leaked portion of the pod's Redis slot-counter occupancy": when the reaper's own unconditional `Shutdown` reclaims a still-registered slot (A1 case) and reports an unclean exit, the §5.2 table says the slot enters `leaked`, yet SPEC-1a keeps a plain member and adds no `leaked:` member. Declined as below the bar: capacity is identical (the plain member stays counted for the pod's life because SPEC-1k's second clause answers every later reaper `Shutdown` unclean), a running slot's `ReportSessionScrub` still feeds the drain ledger (pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/leasecontrol/scrubreport_server.go:80), register row A32 records the keep, and the owner chose "keeps the member on any failure". (2) Filing §10.1.2 step 3 ("The pod accepts only RPCs whose generation matches the value it holds for the session the RPC names", spec/10:40) against the reaper's generation-less `Shutdown`: a refusal keeps the member (fail closed, A32), and the generation does not survive an unbind, so the common `absent` case is unaffected. (3) The A34 operator invariant: the spec documents `maxStartingTimeoutSeconds` (spec/06:255), the 300 s `resuming` watchdog (spec/06:240), and `claimOrphanTimeout` (spec/04:517), but the directive places the invariant where the flags are documented (cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go:829-831 already carries the resume half), so it is non-spec, as the edit-sites and performance shards also recorded.
WATCHOUT: if a later round changes the reaper to act on an unclean `reclaimed` answer, the coherent remedy is to release the slot as a leaked release does (S replaced by `leaked:S`, cardinality unchanged, so the set still never empties) rather than to remove S; that would also stop the reaper re-dialing the pod for S on every pass. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-1a leaked-release sentence and reaper sentences; spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:466,469 (`leaked` "Entered" rows).
WATCHOUT: the reaper removing a stale member can let a pod reach occupancy zero while a live session that holds no member (register row A12, a reservation dropped by a post-restart reseed) still runs; before the reaper the stale member masked that. This widens A12's exposure rather than creating a new defect; do not file it against SPEC-1a. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md edge bullet **A reseed drops leaked members and unpersisted reservations**; SPEC-1a "the set holds another member".
USEFUL [owner.stale-slot-reaper.2026-10-19.review]: its narrowing of SPEC-1k to Shutdown-only records, plus the unclean-implies-held-for-life rows of the §5.2 table, settled that "the last `Shutdown` ... reported an unclean exit" can never be followed by a new entry for the session on that pod.

### [spec.30.review-kubernetes.1]
DECISION: no Kubernetes-idiom finding in spec round 30 — BECAUSE the only staged spec text changed since round 29 is SPEC-1a's stale-slot reaper sentences and the new SPEC-1k, and neither adds a SandboxClaim or Sandbox write, a field manager, a status inbox, a finalizer, a webhook dependency, or a step on a synchronous request path. The reaper is a leader-gated background loop under the existing `lenny-gateway-leader` Lease (cited to §12.5 exactly as §7.2's backlog-resume sentence already cites it, spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:338; Lease defined at spec/12_storage-architecture.md:358). It reaches the adapter through the pod address the gateway already reads with its `get`/`list` grant on `Sandbox` (spec/04_system-components.md:632), and it never runs the occupancy-zero disposition, so it never writes or deletes a claim. Two overlapping leaders during a failover are safe because the "set holds another member" check runs inside the keyed Lua removal — ALTERNATIVES: (1) filing SPEC-1k's in-memory adapter record as lost on an adapter container restart (rejected: rule 11's entries are already in-memory, and the spec defines no adapter-restart path for this to contradict; speculative); (2) filing the `claimOrphanTimeout` coupling below as a spec finding (rejected: SPEC-1a reads true if the chart feeds both processes one value, so the defect is in the non-spec staging).
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: CODE-9 item 6 and **Eligible member.** describe `--slot-reaper-terminal-age` (Helm `gateway.slotReaper.terminalAgeSeconds`) as "the `claimOrphanTimeout` window". SPEC-1a states the reaper waits `claimOrphanTimeout`, which §4.6.1 defines as the WarmPoolController's `--claim-orphan-timeout` (spec/04_system-components.md:517). The two are separate flags in separate Deployments, and the chart renders no `--claim-orphan-timeout` (grep of charts/lenny finds none; cmd/lenny-controller/flags.go:177). They agree only at the 5-minute default. What is true: the gateway uses its own copy of the value. The fix is to render both flags from one chart value, or to state in CODE-9 that they must be set together. The A34 operator invariant (start and resume deadlines do not exceed the reaper age) has to name whichever value the gateway actually reads.
FACT: `git log 85c9ab19a..HEAD -- spec/` is still empty at round 30 (HEAD cf80c3221), so the SPEC-2 Kubernetes baseline of spec.29.review-kubernetes.1 holds unchanged — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:619, :632.
USEFUL [Traps: Kubernetes recheck scope]: holds for the reaper and SPEC-1k too. A round that changes only SPEC-1 needs a recheck of the new sentences alone.

### [spec.30.review-mechanism.1]
DECISION: no mechanism finding on the spec staging in round 30 — BECAUSE I traced SPEC-1a's reaper sentences and SPEC-1k end to end against §5.2 (disposition table, reclaim hold, **Post-recovery rehydration atomicity.** after SPEC-1b), §4.6.1 orphan GC predicate 1, §4.7.1 rules 10 to 15 and the registry critical section, §7.1 **Pod-side reclaim on a failed bind.**, §10.1.2, §12.5, and §28. The reaper's removal gate (clean `Shutdown`, flag present, SCARD > 1) never empties a set, so the occupancy-zero disposition still runs only on the release that empties it; every interleaving of `reapScript` with a late `releaseScript` is serialized by the Lua scripts; rehydration seeds only non-terminal rows, so it cannot resurrect a reaped member — ALTERNATIVES: filing the items below, each rejected as fail-closed residue the owner directive assigns to register rows, or as below the bar.
FACT: SPEC-1k's three anchors match once (spec/04_system-components.md:760 rule 11, :764 rule 15; spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:25). No other spec/ or schemas/ site ties `absent` to a clean exit (grep for "acknowledged clean", "clean exit", `exited_cleanly`); the `SlotReclaimOutcome` enum comments in schemas/lenny-adapter.proto:493-503 cite rules only. EVIDENCE: as cited.
WATCHOUT: §10.1.2 step 3 (spec/10_gateway-internals.md:42) and the §28 one-holder paragraph (spec/28_communication-channels.md:1808) say the pod accepts only RPCs carrying the generation it holds for the session. SPEC-1a's leader `Shutdown` carries none (CODE-9 item 4 calls `adapterclient.Client.Shutdown`, which sets no `coordination_generation`, client.go:981-988). For a still-bound, previously fenced session (the A1 case the **Eligible member.** bullet says the reaper stops), a conforming adapter would refuse it and the reaper keeps the member (A32, fail closed). The tree's adapter does not gate `Shutdown` on generation (pkg/adapter/coordination.go gates only CoordinatorFence and CheckpointBarrier), so it is not a finding now; the non-spec claim "is stopped by the reaper's `Shutdown`" holds only for an unfenced session. EVIDENCE: as cited.
WATCHOUT: A33 makes an overlapping duplicate release (for example a pre-running reclaim's unconditional `Shutdown` still tearing down while a failed `/start`'s compensation arrives) book a permanent `leaked:<sid>`. §5.2 already lets the gateway's reading of a response enter `leaked` independently of the table row the cleanup met (spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:475, "a [§7.1] reclaim the adapter does not answer enters it"), so the "Not entered" cells of the Shutdown-reclaimed rows are not contradicted. Do not re-file it as a (c) against SPEC-1k's lead. EVIDENCE: spec/05:463-475.
OPEN: the caller's delegated A34 decision ("start and resume deadlines do not exceed claimOrphanTimeout", staged where the flags are documented) is not yet staged anywhere. If whoever applies it states the invariant in the spec's §11.3 timeouts table (spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:232-235, :242), note that the spec's own defaults there put `gateway.maxFinalizingTimeoutSeconds` at 600s against `--claim-orphan-timeout` at 300s, and §6.2's `resuming` watchdog is a hard-coded 300s (spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:240). Decide whether a finalize-phase RPC counts as a "start" before writing the invariant, or A34 will not be misconfiguration-only at the defaults.

### [spec.30.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no finding in spec round 30 under the performance and failure-mode lens — BECAUSE the only staged-spec changes since round 29 are SPEC-1a's reaper sentences and the new SPEC-1k (e89383168, 6bf7ca8ab), and neither adds an etcd write (the reaper writes no claim), a watch, or a single-key hot spot. At Tier 3 the reaper's cost is bounded by its own rate: discovery is one cross-tenant `ListPodsWithActiveSlots` plus one `SMEMBERS` per listed pod per pass (about 2,500 per 60 s, about 42/s) and one batched primary-key read over the plain members (about 10,000 ids per pass); releases add at most 50 scripts and 50 `Shutdown` calls per second against the ~6,500/s §12.4 budget. Failure modes: a Postgres failover or Redis reset keeps every member (fail closed, F21/A32); a leader handoff cannot overlap leaders (§12.5 "the two cannot overlap", spec/12_storage-architecture.md:358) and the steps are idempotent anyway — ALTERNATIVES: (1) filing SPEC-1a's "the pod's `rehydrated` flag is present" condition as nullifying the reaper after an outage, because the outage rebuild deletes the flag of every listed pod (non-spec-changes.md:176) and with hour-long sessions per-pod slot traffic is sparse; rejected because a reservation attempt rehydrates before its cap check (pkg/gateway/storage/slotcounter/slotcounter.go:196-198, Reserve retries after -2) and `ClaimSlot` Pass 1 runs `reserveSlot` on every same-tenant claimed pod until one has room (pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/slotclaimer.go:417-469), so the first demand per tenant restores the flags and the next pass reaps; the cost is one pass of lag after first demand, fail closed, and A32 records the absent-flag keep. (2) SPEC-1k's unclean `absent` under overlapping duplicate `Shutdown`s booking extra leaks; rejected, A33 records it and per-replica terminal releases are serialized by `terminalClaimRecord.startRelease` (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/replica_records.go:114-123), so overlaps need two replicas.
WATCHOUT: the non-spec claims "about 12,000 members clear in about 4 minutes" (Stale-slot reaper **Rate.**, summary Decision 23) and A27's "within about 10 minutes" are wall-clock-true only for pods whose `rehydrated` flag has been restored by a reservation or release after the outage; every A27 member from an outage sits on a pod whose flag the rebuild deleted. Capacity is still recovered on first demand plus one pass. A non-spec lens may want the recovery-time wording to say so. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:176, :211; spec-changes.md SPEC-1a "the pod's `rehydrated` flag is present".
WATCHOUT: CODE-9's `ReapStaleSlots` resolves the Sandbox per pod through the gateway's cluster client, which shares the leader replica's client-go limiter (`--cluster-qps` default 100, cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go `cluster-qps`) with that replica's session-start path; a drain at 50 pods/s can take half of it. Non-spec concern only. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go (cluster-qps help text "The session-start path issues 5+ API calls per request").
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: SPEC-1a says the reaper uses `claimOrphanTimeout` (the §4.6.1 value "configurable via `--claim-orphan-timeout`", spec/04_system-components.md:517), but CODE-9 item 6 gives the gateway its own `--slot-reaper-terminal-age` that only defaults to the controller's default, and the chart sets neither, so an operator who tunes the controller flag makes the two diverge. True instead: the chart should render one value into both flags (or CODE-9 should say the two must be set together). Not filed: remedy is non-spec.
OPEN: the caller directive's A34 remedy (state that start and resume deadlines do not exceed `claimOrphanTimeout` where the flags are documented, and rewrite A34 as misconfiguration-only) is not yet staged; register row A34 (non-spec-changes.md:636) still reads as an unconditional fail-open. The spec documents no start or resume deadline flag beside `claimOrphanTimeout` (grep `maxResumingSeconds` finds nothing in spec/), so this belongs to the non-spec loop.
USEFUL [owner.stale-slot-reaper.2026-10-19]: its FACTs (terminated_at stamping, `connectSlot` dial path, leader gate) let me check SPEC-1a's reaper sentences without re-deriving the tree.

### [spec.30.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no reliability finding on the staged spec edits in round 30 — BECAUSE the only staged-edit changes since the converged run (bbee67647) are SPEC-1a's reaper sentences and new SPEC-1k (`git diff bbee67647 HEAD -- <proposal>.spec-changes.md`), and both hold under restart, Lease failover, outage, and duplicate release: the reaper's removal is keyed and atomic with its flag-present and another-member checks, so two overlapping leaders or a reaper racing a late release remove a member at most once and never empty a set; every failure keeps the member (F21, A32); SPEC-1k's in-flight and after-unclean arms make the reaper keep a member whose teardown is unfinished or failed — ALTERNATIVES: (1) filing SPEC-1k's in-flight unclean answer as turning a routine duplicate release into a permanent `leaked:` member. Rejected: the D1/D2 pre-running reclaim sends no `Shutdown` (pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/reclaim.go:79-88; non-spec rows D1 and D2), so the cancel-during-start interleaving has only one `Shutdown`. Same-replica releases are serialized by `startRelease`, so overlapping `Shutdown` calls need a cross-replica duplicate (D6, the reaper against a late release), which is rare and is register row A33. (2) Filing an adapter-process restart as losing SPEC-1k's in-memory records. Rejected: §10.1.4 treats total connection loss as a whole-pod failure (spec/10_gateway-internals.md:62), and the §5.2 reclaim hold has the same in-memory scope today. (3) Filing A34 (a bind still in flight `claimOrphanTimeout` after the terminal write) as a spec gap. Rejected for the spec loop: the spec states no gateway start deadline, and the owner's delegated remedy, an operator invariant that start and resume deadlines do not exceed `claimOrphanTimeout`, belongs where the gateway flags are documented (cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go:829-831 already says this for `--max-resuming-seconds`; DOCS-1 item 11). §7.2 **Exits from `resuming`.** already states that a restore older than `claimOrphanTimeout` loses its pod (spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:443).
FACT: today's adapter answers every non-removing `Shutdown` arm with `exitedCleanly=true` (pkg/adapter/session.go:306-308, :331-334), and a second `Shutdown` that arrives during a first one's teardown takes the fast path and answers `absent`, because the first one deregistered the entry under s.mu before it tore the slot down (session.go:302-305, :336). So SPEC-1k's preamble ("today an `absent` answer reports a clean exit even after ... an unclean exit") is accurate — EVIDENCE: as cited.
FACT: the only spec sites that tie `absent` to a clean exit are §4.7.1 rule 15 (spec/04_system-components.md:764) and §7.1 **Pod-side reclaim on a failed bind.** (spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:25). SPEC-1k stages both. The proto enum comment (schemas/lenny-adapter.proto:499) states no exit flag — EVIDENCE: `/usr/bin/grep` over spec/ and schemas/.
WATCHOUT: A34 is still the fail-open row in non-spec-changes.md:636. The 2026-10-19 delegation rewrites it as misconfiguration-only and stages the start/resume-deadline invariant. If a later spec round sees the invariant missing from spec/, it is not a spec-loop finding unless the fixer chose a spec home — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:636; cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go:831.
USEFUL [owner.stale-slot-reaper.2026-10-19.review]: its narrowing of SPEC-1k to `Shutdown`-teardown records made it unnecessary to re-check the §5.2 table rows "released outside a `Shutdown`" (spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:470-471).

### [spec.30.review-security.1]
DECISION: no security finding on the staged spec edits in round 30 — BECAUSE the only spec-staging changes since round 29 are SPEC-1a's reaper sentences, SPEC-1k (rule 11, rule 15, §7.1 reclaim), Design item 1, and the A27 edge bullet (`git diff bbc747ec9 HEAD -- '*.spec-changes.md'`), and none regresses a control: the leader is an ordinary gateway replica, gateway egress to agent namespaces on TCP 50051 is already allowed (spec/13_security-model.md:275), the `lenny-gateway-leader` Lease citation resolves (§12.5, spec/12_storage-architecture.md:358), the reaper writes no claim and never empties a set, never removes a `leaked:` member, and needs a clean adapter answer before removal; SPEC-1k only turns some `absent` answers unclean, which is stricter (§7.1 reclaim now enters `leaked` more often) — ALTERNATIVES: (1) filing the reaper's clean confirmation as a pod self-report bound; rejected because §5.2's disposition table already makes the adapter's clean-exit flag the source of every release's leaked/clean outcome (spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:465-473), and a concurrent pod is single-tenant, so the reaper trusts nothing the session's own release did not already trust. (2) Filing the outside-`Shutdown` unclean teardown (hold-timeout termination, start-failure handler) whose later `absent` answers clean; rejected because the session's own release removes the member on the same clean answer, so the reaper is no looser than the tree. (3) Filing A34 (bind in flight past `claimOrphanTimeout`) as a fail-open cap; rejected under this lens: the cap bounds same-tenant co-residence, and the caller's directive settles A34 as an operator invariant on start and resume deadlines. (4) Filing the reaper's generation-less `Shutdown` against §10.1.2 step 3's per-session generation check; rejected because a refused `Shutdown` keeps the member (fails closed), and the tree's adapter does not check the generation on `Shutdown` (only `coordination.go` and `sessionseal.go` read it).
FACT: Adapter-side `Shutdown` generation validation does not exist in the tree: `GetCoordinationGeneration()` is read only by pkg/adapter/coordination.go:120,262 and pkg/adapter/sessionseal.go:42,80, although the proto comment says a pod validates it on every RPC. EVIDENCE: schemas/lenny-adapter.proto:1731-1736.
FACT: The reclaim hold keeps an unclean teardown's identifier for the pod's life (spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md table rows at :466 and :469), so no new entry for the session can follow an unclean `Shutdown`, and SPEC-1k's "the last `Shutdown` that removed the session's entry" is always the unclean one. EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:477 ("While the identifier is held the adapter admits no request ...").
WATCHOUT: SPEC-1k's lead says only the outside-`Shutdown` "Not entered" rows stay true. In the A33 overlap, a duplicate release whose `absent` answer arrives while another `Shutdown`'s teardown is in flight records `leaked:S`, even when that teardown later completes clean and the §5.2 row "Every act returns without error" says `leaked` is "Not entered". This fails closed and is not a security finding. A mechanism or single-source lens should decide whether it contradicts the table's keying on how the cleanup ends. EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:465; spec-changes SPEC-1k rule 11 replacement.
USEFUL [spec.28.review-security.1]: its Bounded-cohort and Pass 1 join alternatives settled the question of whether the reaper's freed slot relaxes a reuse bound.

### [spec.32.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: the reaper-vs-seal overlap is closed by an operator invariant staged as new SPEC-1l, one in-place substitution in §7.1 **Seal-and-export invariant:** ("`maxWorkspaceSealDurationSeconds` ... must not exceed `claimOrphanTimeout`"), plus a DOCS-1 item on docs/reference/configuration.md's `pool.maxWorkspaceSealDurationSeconds` Description cell — BECAUSE it is the remedy the owner chose for A34 (start and resume deadlines do not exceed `claimOrphanTimeout`), it adds no mechanism and no timing window, and §7.1 is where the seal window is defined — ALTERNATIVES: (1) widen the reaper's eligibility to `claimOrphanTimeout` plus the seal window: a second timing window the directive bars, and it delays recovery by 5 more minutes at the defaults; (2) skip a session until its seal outcome is recorded: a replica crash mid-seal leaves the member stale forever, which is the case the reaper exists for; (3) an adapter-side refusal of `Shutdown` while a seal export is in flight: `Shutdown` is unconditional for every caller and the backoff gaps hold no RPC, so it would not protect the workspace; (4) a strict inequality with a lowered seal default (for example 240s): cascades into spec/07, spec/16 alert row, two docs pages, the tree constant, flag text, and the chart for a sub-millisecond residue; (5) a gateway startup check comparing `--workspace-seal-max-duration-seconds` with `--slot-reaper-terminal-age`: inconsistent with the owner's text-only A34 remedy and checks only the platform value, not a per-pool one.
WATCHOUT: at equal defaults (300s and 300s) a residue remains of the delay between the terminal write and the seal's start (AfterTerminalWrite runs CancelResumeAttempt, then the seal, milliseconds after the Update) plus clock skew between the DB-stamped `terminated_at` and the gateway's seal deadline; a reaper pass landing in that window cuts only the last milliseconds of a seal already at its timeout. Accepted under the owner's "do not design for every corner case" directive; do not re-file it as a strict-margin finding without a new owner decision — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_hook.go:57-62; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/seal.go:119-122
FACT: the seal starts in the post-terminal hook after the terminal Update and runs under `context.WithTimeout(ctx, s.sealMaxDuration)` with the deadline taken from the seal's own start, so the seal can still be retrying `claimOrphanTimeout` after `terminated_at` whenever the seal window is at least that long — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_hook.go:54-75; seal.go:119-122; cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go:1143-1145
DEFERRED [0093_...implementation-checklist.md]: S1 says "Lands the staged edits SPEC-1a to SPEC-1k"; with SPEC-1l staged it must read "SPEC-1a to SPEC-1l".
OPEN: the non-spec loop's A34 rewrite stages the start and resume invariant "where the flags are documented"; that site should cite §7.1 (SPEC-1l) for the seal window rather than restate it, and decide whether `--workspace-seal-max-duration-seconds` usage text names the bound as `--max-resuming-seconds` already does.

### [spec.32.review-mechanism.1]
DECISION: filed one finding: SPEC-1a's reaper treats a member as stale once its session has been terminal for longer than `claimOrphanTimeout`, but the post-terminal hook seals and exports the workspace AFTER the terminal write and BEFORE the release, for up to `maxWorkspaceSealDurationSeconds` (default 300 s, deployer-tunable via `gateway.workspaceSealMaxDurationSeconds`). A seal still retrying past `claimOrphanTimeout` (a MinIO outage, the `WorkspaceSealStuck` case) has its slot torn down by the reaper's unconditional `Shutdown` (rule 12 removes the entry and the workspace), so the export cannot complete, which contradicts the §7.1 **Seal-and-export invariant:** ("always exported ... before the pod is released"). BECAUSE no register row, edge bullet, or staged constraint covers it, and the loss is session output rather than fail-closed capacity — ALTERNATIVES: filing it as a variant of the refuted A34 item (rejected: A34 is a bind in flight and is recorded; this case is unrecorded and loses data); a register row (rejected: the directive admits register rows only for fail-closed residue).
FACT: hook order is terminal Update (stamps `terminated_at`) -> `CancelResumeAttempt` -> `sealWorkspace` (real-time ctx of `sealMaxDuration`) -> drain -> release — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_hook.go:54-75; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/seal.go:101-163; failWorkspaceSeal's relabel comment "from is terminal" seal.go:221. Spec mirrors it: spec/29_communication-scenarios.md steps 10-12 (terminal state, then seal, then `Shutdown`).
FACT: `Checkpointer.Seal` drives the bound pod adapter's checkpoint of the session's slot workspace (pkg/gateway/checkpoint/checkpointer/checkpointer.go:396-420); the seal holds no §5.2 slot serialization, so nothing orders it against another `Shutdown`.
WATCHOUT: at the defaults both windows are 300 s; the overlap is then only the delay between the terminal Update and the seal start, but a deployer raising the seal window or lowering `--claim-orphan-timeout`/`--slot-reaper-terminal-age` makes it routine. A remedy stating "seal window at or below claimOrphanTimeout" still leaves that delta; it needs a strict bound or a default change.
USEFUL [spec.30.review-mechanism.1]: its trace of the reaper against §5.2, §4.6.1, and §4.7.1 let me skip re-deriving the Lua serialization and rule-11/12 interactions.

### [spec.32.introspection.1]
DECISION: the introspection pass after spec round 32 returned `redesign`, and the run stopped with status `stopped-redesign` at round 32 (17 full-pool sweeps, 4 findings fixed this run). Its diagnosis: one invariant, that the stale-slot reaper's age must outlast every activity that can still act on a session's slot after its terminal write, was being patched one facet per round at three sites: register row A34 (the bind still in flight), SPEC-1l plus a spec-changes edge bullet (the seal window), and an OPEN on the finalize window (spec.30.review-mechanism.1). Each patch stated its own window against `claimOrphanTimeout`, the gateway knob `--slot-reaper-terminal-age` diverged from the controller's `--claim-orphan-timeout`, and the round-30 and round-32 DEFERREDs on that split stayed open — BECAUSE the rounds kept confirming one facet at a time (spec.32.review-mechanism.1 confirmed the seal facet after spec.30 left the bind and finalize facets open) — ALTERNATIVES: continuing the loop (rejected by the pass: each round finds the next facet).
FACT: the round-32 fix (spec.32.fix-G1.1, spec.32.fix-design-G1.1, spec.32.follow-up-fix.1) staged SPEC-1l on §7.1 and the seal edge bullet, and left four DEFERREDs (S1 range, DOCS-1 reference row, register row A35, DOCS-1 item 11 sentence) and two OPENs (gateway startup refusal, the A34 flag-site staging). The remedy below disposes of each.

### [remedy.reaper-age-table.2026-10-19]
DECISION: under the owner's delegation of 2026-10-19 (simplest fail-closed mechanism, workable at Tier 3 where sessions last an hour or more, recovery of 10 to 20 minutes acceptable, no corner-case over-coverage, no timing heuristic beyond the orphan timeout), the post-terminal timing is one table, one invariant, and one value. The non-spec **Post-terminal slot activity table** (rows T1 to T7, derived from the tree at 22736c0ca) is the only rule site; the **Invariant** is `claimOrphanTimeout` ≥ the largest of `--max-finalizing-state-timeout-seconds` (T1), `--max-resuming-seconds` (T3), and `--workspace-seal-max-duration-seconds` (T4) plus one second; SPEC-1a states it once in the spec; CODE-9 items 6 and 7 render one chart value, `controller.claimOrphanTimeoutSeconds`, into both flags and check the invariant at gateway startup and chart rendering — BECAUSE three sites each stating one window drifted, and two copies of the timeout could differ — ALTERNATIVES: see the **Settled** entry **Post-terminal timing**.
DECISION: the shared default rises from 5 to 15 minutes (summary Decision 24). The largest bounded window is 600 seconds (the T1 launch bound) and the seal and restore windows equal the old 300-second default, so 5 minutes fails the invariant. 15 minutes leaves 300 seconds of margin and a recovery time of about 20 minutes, inside the owner's range. Raising the timeout also delays the controller's orphan GC by 10 minutes on every GC-bounded departure (register **GC** backstop), which the owner's guidance accepts — BECAUSE no fail-open or seal-loss residue is accepted at a valid configuration — ALTERNATIVES: 10 minutes (meets the invariant with the same 600-second window only at equality, which the one-second margin refuses).
DECISION: the launch onto a slot had no gateway deadline (T1: `handleStart` runs `launchOnPod` on the request context, and `MaterializeDelegatedChild` runs `startOnPod` on the tool call's context), so CODE-9 item 7 bounds it by `--max-finalizing-state-timeout-seconds`, taken before the caller's read of the session row. On a concurrent-workspace pool the launch performs the materialization and setup that `finalizing` performs on an exclusive pool, and §6.2 keeps that value at or above `setupTimeoutSeconds` — BECAUSE the bound is the simplest treatment that makes the launch's window end before `terminated_at` plus the timeout — ALTERNATIVES: bounding the launch by `claimOrphanTimeout` itself (leaves the writer's stamp-to-commit gap uncovered); `--max-ready-state-timeout-seconds` (300 seconds can cut a launch whose setup uses the full `setupTimeoutSeconds`).
FACT: finalize does no pod work on a concurrent-workspace pool: `prepareAtFinalize` returns nil when `match.MaxConcurrentSessions > 1` (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/finalize.go, the comment "a concurrent-workspace pool reserves a slot at create and materializes + launches it together at /start"), so the 600-second finalize window is not in the invariant (row T2). This closes the finalize OPEN of spec.30.review-mechanism.1 — EVIDENCE: finalize.go `prepareAtFinalize`.
FACT: the seal starts milliseconds after the terminal write: `AfterTerminalWrite` runs right after the writer's Update (mcptools.go and usage.go start the hook goroutine immediately after the write), and its first step `CancelResumeAttempt` only cancels a context (`resumeAttempts.cancel`); `sealWorkspace` bounds itself by `sealMaxDuration` from its own start — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/terminal_hook.go `AfterTerminalWrite`; resume_driver.go `CancelResumeAttempt`; seal.go `sealWorkspace`.
FACT: a resume attempt sends no pod RPC before its `resuming` entry write, and the entry hook resets the attempt's bound to `resumingCap()` at that write, so its window starts before any terminal write that ends the incarnation — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_driver.go `entryHook.run` ("the hook the binder calls after its claim and before any RPC reaches the pod"), `attemptBound.reset`.
FACT: the combined create-and-start path writes its row with `store.Create` after `startOnPod` returns, so its launch has no row the reaper can find terminal — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/create_and_start.go (`startOnPod` before `s.store.Create`).
FACT: the chart renders no `--claim-orphan-timeout`, `--max-finalizing-state-timeout-seconds`, or `--max-resuming-seconds`; it renders `--workspace-seal-max-duration-seconds` from `gateway.workspaceSealMaxDurationSeconds` when that value is positive — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/templates/gateway-deployment.yaml; charts/lenny/templates/controller-deployment.yaml; cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go.
DECISION: register row A34 is rewritten as misconfiguration-only (the owner's delegated decision): at a configuration the chart renders and the gateway accepts, a launch or restore can meet the reaper's `Shutdown` only when gateway replica clocks disagree by more than the margin. New row A35 records the same condition for the seal, and the spec-changes seal edge bullet is a pointer to it. SPEC-1l now stages the default change (§4.6.1 predicate 1 and the §11.3 row); its earlier §7.1 seal substitution is withdrawn, because SPEC-1a states the one bound — BECAUSE one spec statement replaces per-window statements.
DECISION: recovery-time numbers have one home, the **Stale-slot reaper** **Recovery time.** bullet; register rows A27 and the **Counted** backstop, summary Decision 23, and DOCS-1 items 9 and 11 cite it. The bullet also states the absent-`rehydrated`-flag lag of spec.30.review-performance.1's WATCHOUT.
DECISION: the design premise names the A33 exception. Non-spec Design paragraph 1 and the **Resource-to-owner table** lead now say a duplicate release is a no-op apart from row A13 and a release whose `Shutdown` is answered `absent` with an unclean exit (row A33).
DECISION: R11 names the live binding `MarkBudgetUnrecoverable` leaves on an `awaiting_client_action` row (and on the `resume_pending` row `enterResumePending` carries it to), which full_revoke step 4(b) releases as R5 or R6. `ReleaseRevokedBinding` releases it because `MarkBudgetUnrecoverable` and `enterResumePending` leave `coordination_generation` unchanged — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationbudget/adapters.go `MarkBudgetUnrecoverable`; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go `enterResumePending`; terminal_binding.go generation check.
DECISION: Decision 15's universal is false and is corrected. The coordination Sweeper renews the lease of every session its replica binds, whatever the row's state other than podless `suspended` (pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go `sweepRow`), so a peer can take a bound `resume_pending` or `awaiting_client_action` row only after that replica's renewals fail for a whole lease TTL; the peer's `Sweeper.takeover` of a recovering row or its resume attempt's `resuming` write then raises the generation, and the replica's binding becomes stale with a resume-family `fromState` with no running-state takeover. The stale replica still evicts without a release (CODE-5 unchanged); a pod only it held goes to the orphan GC (exclusive) or stays counted until the reaper (slot), register row A2.
CORRECTS [non-spec.1.fix-design-G3.1 UNVERIFIED "whether a resume driver on replica B can take a `resume_pending` row ..."]: yes, after A's lease lapses; see the Decision 15 entry above.
CORRECTS [non-spec.1.fix-design-G3.1 OPEN "R11's binding column ... omits the `MarkBudgetUnrecoverable` row"]: fixed in R11.
CORRECTS [spec.30.review-applicability.1 OPEN, spec.30.review-edit-sites.1 OPEN, spec.30.review-performance.1 OPEN, spec.30.review-mechanism.1 OPEN, spec.30.review-docs-alignment.1 DEFERRED, spec.30.review-citations.1 DEFERRED, spec.30.review-kubernetes.1 DEFERRED, spec.30.review-performance.1 DEFERRED]: closed by one chart value feeding both flags, the **Invariant** with its checks, and the A34 rewrite.
CORRECTS [spec.32.fix-G1.1 DEFERRED S1 range; spec.32.fix-design-G1.1 DEFERRED S1 range]: S1 reads "SPEC-1a to SPEC-1l".
CORRECTS [spec.32.fix-G1.1 DEFERRED DOCS-1 reference row for `pool.maxWorkspaceSealDurationSeconds`]: withdrawn. The §7.1 seal sentence it mirrored is no longer staged; DOCS-1 item 11 states the invariant beside the shared value, naming the seal window.
CORRECTS [spec.32.fix-G1.1 OPEN gateway startup refusal; spec.32.fix-design-G1.1 OPEN A34 flag-site staging]: the gateway refuses to start (CODE-9 item 7), and the flag descriptions cite the **Invariant**.
CORRECTS [spec.32.follow-up-fix.1 DEFERRED register row A35; DEFERRED DOCS-1 item 11 seal sentence]: row A35 added; item 11 states the invariant.
CORRECTS [spec.32.review-single-source.1 UNVERIFIED rule 11's in-flight arm]: the arm is reachable. `Shutdown`'s removing arm deregisters the entry under `s.mu` before it tears the slot down, so a second `Shutdown` during that teardown takes the no-entry path and answers `absent` (spec.30.review-reliability.1 FACT; pkg/adapter/session.go `Shutdown`).
WATCHOUT: no site other than the **Post-terminal slot activity table** and its **Invariant** may state a window against `claimOrphanTimeout`; SPEC-1a states the rule, and CODE-9 items 6 and 7 enforce it. A new post-terminal activity gets a row first.
